**Book-overview note:** *Extends.* (1) Add to the Echo Table: φορτίον ("burden") 11:30 ↔ 23:4 and βαρύς ("heavy") 23:4, 23 only; ἐπισυνάγω ("to gather together") 23:37 → 24:31 only; ἐκχυννόμενον ("poured out") 23:35 → 26:28 only (this strengthens Trap 3's corrective with a verbal link); ἀπ' ἄρτι ("from now on") 23:39; 26:29, 64 only; ῥαββί ("Rabbi") 23:7–8 → 26:25, 49 (Judas alone uses it). (2) Add Mic 6:8 → 23:23 (moderate) and the Isa 31:5 cluster → 23:37 (moderate) as candidates. (3) The Jer 22:5 row (moderate) could rise to moderate–high: Jer 7:11 is already quoted at 21:13, and 21:13's "my house" becomes "your house" at 23:38. Flag that ἔρημος ("desolate") at 23:38 is omitted by 𝔓⁷⁷ᵛⁱᵈ B L. (4) Arc Map: 23:34 makes Jesus the sender of prophets, where Luke has "the Wisdom of God" — a Christological datum the Christological Trajectory could use `[I]`, moderate.

---

## 45. Matthew 24:1–35 — The Temple, the End, and the Coming of the Son of Man ⭐

**Position.** *Q1:* Jesus has just said "your house is left to you desolate" and "you will not see me from now on" (23:38–39) `[T]`. *Q2:* this unit exists here because the disciples hear the verdict on the house and ask about it. Jesus leaves the ἱερόν ("temple", 24:1), they show him its buildings, and he says not one stone will be left on another (24:2) `[T]`. On the Mount of Olives they ask two things: πότε ταῦτα ἔσται ("when will these things happen") and τί τὸ σημεῖον τῆς σῆς παρουσίας καὶ συντελείας τοῦ αἰῶνος ("what will be the sign of your coming, and of the end of the age", 24:3) `[T]`. The unit answers both, and ends with an oath on "this generation" and on his words (24:34–35) `[T]`. It could not stand earlier: it needs the temple's sentence (23:38) and the claim that the Son of David is David's Lord at God's right hand (22:44), whose coming it now describes `[I]`, high.

**Structure.**

| Verses | Section |
|---|---|
| 24:1–3 | Leaving the temple; the stones; the two-part question on the Mount of Olives |
| 24:4–14 | "See that no one misleads you": birth pangs, hatred, false prophets, endurance; the gospel to all the nations, "and then the end will come" |
| 24:15–28 | The abomination of desolation; flight; great tribulation; false christs; the coming like lightning |
| 24:29–31 | After the tribulation: sun, moon, stars; the sign of the Son of Man; the tribes mourn; the elect gathered |
| 24:32–35 | The fig tree; "this generation"; "my words will not pass away" |

The discourse opens as the first did. 5:1: καθίσαντος αὐτοῦ προσῆλθαν αὐτῷ οἱ μαθηταὶ αὐτοῦ ("when he sat down, his disciples came to him") `[T]`. 24:3: Καθημένου δὲ αὐτοῦ ἐπὶ τοῦ Ὄρους τῶν Ἐλαιῶν προσῆλθον αὐτῷ οἱ μαθηταί ("as he was sitting on the Mount of Olives, the disciples came to him") `[T]`. Seated on a mountain, the disciples approaching: the first and last discourses share the setting `[I]`, moderate–high (overview's mountain frame). Three markers carry the order: τὸ τέλος ("the end", 24:6, 13, 14), τότε ("then", 24:9, 10, 14, 16, 21, 23, 30 twice) and Εὐθέως δὲ μετά ("immediately after", 24:29) `[T]`. The first warning is βλέπετε μή τις ὑμᾶς πλανήσῃ ("see to it that no one misleads you", 24:4), and πλανάω ("to mislead") returns at 24:5, 11, 24 `[T]`, verified.

**Text-first findings.**

- **The temple is "thrown down", the word of 5:17.** οὐ μὴ ἀφεθῇ ὧδε λίθος ἐπὶ λίθον ὃς οὐ καταλυθήσεται ("not one stone here will be left upon another, which will not be torn down", 24:2) `[T]`. καταλύω ("to tear down, abolish") stands at 5:17 (twice); 24:2; 26:61; 27:40 only (SBLGNT) `[T]`, verified. He did not come καταλῦσαι ("to abolish") the Law (5:17); the temple will be καταλυθήσεται ("torn down", 24:2); the witnesses and the mockers twist it into his own threat, "I am able to destroy the temple" (26:61; 27:40) `[T]`. *Synthetic*: moderate–high.
- **παρουσία ("coming") is Matthew's word.** It stands at 24:3, 27, 37, 39 and nowhere else in the Gospels (SBLGNT, whole NT) `[T]`, verified. The disciples' second question uses the parables' phrase: συντέλεια τοῦ αἰῶνος ("end of the age") stands at 13:39, 40, 49; 24:3; 28:20 only `[T]`, verified (overview). The last answer to "the end of the age" is 28:20, "I am with you always, to the end of the age" `[T]`.
- **"Not yet the end."** δεῖ γὰρ γενέσθαι, ἀλλ' οὔπω ἐστὶν τὸ τέλος ("for those things must take place, but that is not yet the end", 24:6); πάντα δὲ ταῦτα ἀρχὴ ὠδίνων ("all these things are merely the beginning of birth pangs", 24:8) `[T]`. τέλος ("end") stands at 10:22; 17:25; 24:6, 13, 14; 26:58 (SBLGNT) `[T]`, verified. ὁ δὲ ὑπομείνας εἰς τέλος οὗτος σωθήσεται ("the one who endures to the end, he will be saved", 24:13) repeats 10:22 verbatim; ὑπομένω ("to endure") stands only there `[T]`, verified (overview). 24:9 also repeats 10:22's ἔσεσθε μισούμενοι ὑπὸ πάντων ("you will be hated by all"), and adds τῶν ἐθνῶν ("the nations") `[T]`. The mission discourse said "all"; the last discourse says "all the nations" `[T]`.
- **The gospel goes to the nations before the end.** κηρυχθήσεται τοῦτο τὸ εὐαγγέλιον τῆς βασιλείας ἐν ὅλῃ τῇ οἰκουμένῃ εἰς μαρτύριον πᾶσιν τοῖς ἔθνεσιν, καὶ τότε ἥξει τὸ τέλος ("this gospel of the kingdom shall be preached in the whole world as a testimony to all the nations, and then the end will come", 24:14) `[T]`. τὸ εὐαγγέλιον τῆς βασιλείας ("the gospel of the kingdom") stands at 4:23; 9:35; 24:14 only (SBLGNT) `[T]`, verified: the summaries of Jesus' own Galilean preaching. εἰς μαρτύριον ("as a testimony") stands at 8:4; 10:18; 24:14 only `[T]`, verified: to the priests (8:4), to governors and "the nations" (10:18), to "all the nations" (24:14) `[T]`. The widening is in the wording `[I]`, high. Mark 13:10 has "first the gospel must be preached to all the nations"; Matthew adds "and then the end will come" `[T]`.
- **Love grows cold.** καὶ διὰ τὸ πληθυνθῆναι τὴν ἀνομίαν ψυγήσεται ἡ ἀγάπη τῶν πολλῶν ("because lawlessness is increased, the love of the many will grow cold", 24:12) `[T]`. ἀγάπη ("love") stands only here in Matthew (SBLGNT) `[T]`, verified. ἀνομία ("lawlessness") stands at 7:23; 13:41; 23:28; 24:12 (overview) `[T]`. The commandment to love (ἀγαπάω, 22:37, 39) is the measure; its failure is the mark of the time `[I]`, moderate. 24:10–12 is Matthew's alone (Mark 13:13 goes straight to "hated by all") `[T]`.
- **"Spoken through Daniel the prophet"; the reader's aside.** Ὅταν οὖν ἴδητε τὸ βδέλυγμα τῆς ἐρημώσεως τὸ ῥηθὲν διὰ Δανιὴλ τοῦ προφήτου ἑστὸς ἐν τόπῳ ἁγίῳ, ὁ ἀναγινώσκων νοείτω ("when you see the abomination of desolation which was spoken of through Daniel the prophet, standing in the holy place — let the reader understand", 24:15) `[T]`. τὸ ῥηθὲν διὰ … τοῦ προφήτου ("what was spoken through … the prophet") is the wording of the narrator's fulfilment formulae (1:22; 2:15, 17, 23; 4:14; 8:17; 12:17; 13:35; 21:4; 27:9), here without πληρόω ("to fulfil") and in Jesus' mouth `[T]`. The fulfilment is still to come `[I]`, moderate–high. Mark has neither Daniel's name nor "holy place"; he has ἑστηκότα ὅπου οὐ δεῖ ("standing where it ought not", Mark 13:14) `[T]`. ἐν τόπῳ ἁγίῳ ("in the holy place") is a Levitical phrase in Swete (Exod 29:31; Lev 6:16, 26, 27; and others) `[T]`. The aside ὁ ἀναγινώσκων νοείτω ("let the reader understand") stands only here in Matthew `[T]`, verified; it is shared with Mark 13:14 `[T]`. ἀναγινώσκω ("to read") is otherwise always Jesus' "have you not read?" (12:3, 5; 19:4; 21:16, 42; 22:31) `[T]`, verified.
- **The tribulation and Daniel 12:1.** ἔσται γὰρ τότε θλῖψις μεγάλη οἵα οὐ γέγονεν ἀπ' ἀρχῆς κόσμου ἕως τοῦ νῦν οὐδ' οὐ μὴ γένηται ("for then there will be a great tribulation, such as has not occurred since the beginning of the world until now, nor ever will", 24:21) `[T]`. θλῖψις ("tribulation") stands at 13:21; 24:9, 21, 29 (SBLGNT) `[T]`, verified. See Tool 11.
- **The coming is from the east, like lightning.** ὥσπερ γὰρ ἡ ἀστραπὴ ἐξέρχεται ἀπὸ ἀνατολῶν καὶ φαίνεται ἕως δυσμῶν ("for just as the lightning comes from the east and flashes even to the west", 24:27) `[T]`. ἀπὸ ἀνατολῶν ("from the east") stands at 2:1; 8:11; 24:27 only (SBLGNT) `[T]`, verified: the magi "from the east" (2:1), the many who come "from east and west" to recline with Abraham (8:11), and the coming of the Son of Man (24:27) `[T]`. ἀνατολή ("east") with δυσμή ("west") stands only at 8:11 and 24:27 `[T]`, verified. ἀστραπή ("lightning") stands only at 24:27 and 28:3, where the angel at the tomb has an appearance ὡς ἀστραπή ("like lightning") `[T]`, verified. *Synthetic*: moderate.
- **The sign of the Son of Man.** τότε φανήσεται τὸ σημεῖον τοῦ υἱοῦ τοῦ ἀνθρώπου ἐν τῷ οὐρανῷ ("then the sign of the Son of Man will appear in the sky", 24:30) `[T]`. Matthew alone has it (Mark 13:26; Luke 21:27) `[T]`. σημεῖον ("sign") stands in 9 verses (SBLGNT) `[T]`, verified. Twice the Pharisees asked for a sign from heaven and were given only "the sign of Jonah" (12:38–39; 16:1–4) `[T]`. The disciples ask for "the sign" (24:3); false christs give "great signs" (24:24); the true sign appears "in heaven" (24:30) `[T]`. The last σημεῖον ("sign") is Judas' kiss (26:48) `[T]`. The sign "from heaven" refused in 12 and 16 is given at the end `[I]`, moderate.
- **The elect gathered.** ἀποστελεῖ τοὺς ἀγγέλους αὐτοῦ μετὰ σάλπιγγος μεγάλης, καὶ ἐπισυνάξουσιν τοὺς ἐκλεκτοὺς αὐτοῦ ἐκ τῶν τεσσάρων ἀνέμων ἀπ' ἄκρων οὐρανῶν ἕως τῶν ἄκρων αὐτῶν ("he will send forth his angels with a great trumpet, and they will gather together his elect from the four winds, from one end of the sky to the other", 24:31) `[T]`. ἐπισυνάγω ("to gather together") stands only at 23:37 and 24:31 `[T]`, verified (unit 44). ἐκλεκτός ("elect") stands at 22:14; 24:22, 24, 31 only `[T]`, verified (unit 42). The trumpet and "from end of heaven to end of heaven" are Matthew's (see Synoptic check) `[T]`.
- **The fig tree comes back, and heaven and earth pass.** Ἀπὸ δὲ τῆς συκῆς μάθετε τὴν παραβολήν ("Now learn the parable from the fig tree", 24:32) `[T]`. συκῆ ("fig tree") stands at 21:19–21 and 24:32 only; φύλλον ("leaf") at 21:19 and 24:32 only `[T]`, verified (unit 42). The fig tree that withered is now a sign of nearness `[I]`, moderate. ὁ οὐρανὸς καὶ ἡ γῆ παρελεύσεται, οἱ δὲ λόγοι μου οὐ μὴ παρέλθωσιν ("Heaven and earth will pass away, but my words will not pass away", 24:35) `[T]`. ὁ οὐρανὸς καὶ ἡ γῆ ("heaven and earth") with παρέρχομαι ("to pass away") stands at 5:18 and 24:35 only (SBLGNT) `[T]`, verified. At 5:18 not an iota of the Law will pass ἕως ἂν παρέλθῃ ὁ οὐρανὸς καὶ ἡ γῆ ("until heaven and earth pass away") `[T]`. At 24:35 Jesus says the same of "my words" `[T]`. His words are given the permanence he gave the Law `[I]`, high.

**Synoptic check.** Matthew follows Mark 13:1–31 closely in order `[T]`. Additions and changes with weight: the question about τῆς σῆς παρουσίας καὶ συντελείας τοῦ αἰῶνος ("your coming and the end of the age", 24:3; Mark 13:4 has only "when all these things are about to be accomplished") `[T]`; ἐγώ εἰμι ὁ χριστός ("I am the Christ", 24:5; Mark 13:6 ἐγώ εἰμι, "I am") `[T]`; 24:10–12 (stumbling, betrayal, false prophets, lawlessness, love grown cold) `[T]`; "and then the end will come" (24:14) `[T]`; Daniel named and "the holy place" (24:15) `[T]`; "nor on a Sabbath" (24:20) `[T]`; 24:26–28 (the lightning, the corpse and the vultures; double tradition, Luke 17:24, 37) `[T]`; "the sign of the Son of Man" and "all the tribes of the earth will mourn" (24:30) `[T]`; ἐπὶ τῶν νεφελῶν τοῦ οὐρανοῦ ("on the clouds of heaven", 24:30) for Mark's ἐν νεφέλαις ("in clouds") `[S: audit]`; μετὰ σάλπιγγος μεγάλης ("with a great trumpet", 24:31) `[T]`; and ἀπ' ἄκρων οὐρανῶν ἕως τῶν ἄκρων αὐτῶν ("from one end of heaven to the other") for Mark's ἀπ' ἄκρου γῆς ἕως ἄκρου οὐρανοῦ ("from the end of the earth to the end of heaven", Mark 13:27) `[T]`. Mark's "in those days … that tribulation" (Mark 13:24) becomes Εὐθέως ("immediately", 24:29) `[T]`.

**Tool 11.**

**Dan 9:27; 11:31; 12:11 → 24:15** *(high — named)*. *Source context:* Daniel's seventy weeks end with one who "makes a strong covenant with the many for one week"; in the middle of it he "puts an end to sacrifice and offering, and on the wing of abominations comes one who makes desolate" (9:27, WLC שִׁקּוּצִים מְשֹׁמֵם) `[T]`. 11:31: forces "profane the sanctuary, take away the regular burnt offering, and set up the abomination that makes desolate" `[T]`. 12:11: from the time the burnt offering is taken away and the abomination set up, 1,290 days (WLC שִׁקּוּץ שֹׁמֵם; lemmas 8251, 8074 gated) `[T]`. *Text:* τὸ βδέλυγμα τῆς ἐρημώσεως ("the abomination of desolation"), with both articles, stands in Swete only at OG Dan 12:11, and in Rahlfs only at OG Dan 12:11 `[T]`, verified. Without articles, βδέλυγμα ἐρημώσεως ("an abomination of desolation") stands at OG 11:31, Theodotion 12:11 and 1 Macc 1:54 (Swete) `[T]`, verified. 9:27 has the plural βδέλυγμα τῶν ἐρημώσεων ("abomination of the desolations") in both OG and Theodotion `[T]`. *Nearest text:* OG Dan 12:11, on the articles; Mark 13:14 has the same phrase, so the choice is inherited `[T]`. *Book usage:* Daniel's Son of Man (Dan 7:13) runs from 10:23 to 26:64 (overview); this is the only place Daniel is named `[T]`. *OT-to-OT:* Dan 9:27 is the chapter of "a covenant for the many", which the audit raised as a rival for 26:28 `[S: audit]`. The desolation of the house in Jer 22:5 (εἰς ἐρήμωσιν, "into a desolation") supplies the same noun, and 23:38 has just spoken of the house left ἔρημος ("desolate") `[T]`. *What it adds:* the sentence of 23:38 is given a name from Daniel; the desolation of the house is the profanation of the sanctuary `[I]`, moderate–high. (Note: ἔρημος at 23:38 is omitted by 𝔓⁷⁷ᵛⁱᵈ B L.)

**Dan 12:1 → 24:21** *(moderate–high — new as a row)*. *Source context:* "at that time shall arise Michael, the great prince … and there shall be a time of trouble such as never has been since there was a nation till that time; but at that time your people shall be delivered, everyone whose name shall be found written in the book" (WLC עֵת צָרָה; lemma 6869 gated) `[T]`. The next verse is the resurrection of "many who sleep in the dust" to everlasting life and to everlasting contempt (12:2) `[T]`. *Text:* Theodotion θλῖψις οἵα οὐ γέγονεν ἀφ' ἧς γεγένηται ἔθνος … ἐν τῷ καιρῷ ἐκείνῳ σωθήσεται ὁ λαός σου ("tribulation such as has not been since there was a nation … at that time your people will be saved") `[T]`. OG: ἡμέρα θλίψεως, οἵα οὐκ ἐγενήθη ("a day of tribulation such as has not been") `[T]`. οἵα οὐ γέγονεν ("such as has not been") stands in Swete only at Theodotion Dan 9:12 and 12:1 `[T]`, verified; Rahlfs the same `[T]`. Matthew's θλῖψις … οἵα οὐ γέγονεν ("tribulation … such as has not been", 24:21) is nearest **Theodotion** `[T]`. Mark 13:19 has the same words `[T]`, so this is inherited. Matthew's next verse adds the saving: οὐκ ἂν ἐσώθη πᾶσα σάρξ ("no life would have been saved", 24:22), and 24:13 σωθήσεται ("will be saved") `[T]`; Theodotion has σωθήσεται ("will be saved") in the same verse `[T]`. *What it adds:* Daniel names the tribulation, the deliverance of those written in the book, and then the resurrection to life and to contempt; 25:46 will close the discourse with Dan 12:2 `[I]`, moderate–high.

**Isa 13:10; 34:4 (with Joel 2:10) → 24:29** *(high)*. *Source context:* Isa 13 is the oracle against Babylon, "the day of the LORD … cruel, with wrath" (13:9); the stars, sun and moon give no light (13:10) `[T]`. Isa 34 is judgement on the nations and Edom; "all the host of heaven shall rot away, and the skies roll up like a scroll" (34:4) `[T]`. *Text:* Swete Isa 13:10 σκοτισθήσεται τοῦ ἡλίου ἀνατέλλοντος, καὶ ἡ σελήνη οὐ δώσει τὸ φῶς αὐτῆς ("the sun will be darkened at its rising, and the moon will not give its light") `[T]`. Swete Isa 34:4 πᾶσαι αἱ δυνάμεις τῶν οὐρανῶν ("all the powers of the heavens") and πάντα τὰ ἄστρα πεσεῖται ὡς φύλλα ἐξ ἀμπέλου, καὶ ὡς πίπτει φύλλα ἀπὸ συκῆς ("all the stars will fall like leaves from a vine, and as leaves fall from a fig tree") `[T]`. Rahlfs the same in both `[T]`. Matthew's τὸ φέγγος αὐτῆς ("its light", 24:29) is not Isaiah's φῶς ("light"); φέγγος ("light, radiance") stands with sun, moon and stars at Joel 2:10 (Swete and Rahlfs, τὸ φέγγος αὐτῶν, "their light") `[T]`, verified. Mark 13:24 also has φέγγος ("light") `[T]`. *Nearest text:* a Greek composite (Isa 13:10 + Isa 34:4 + Joel's word) `[I]`. *OT-to-OT:* **Isa 34:4 has the falling stars and the fig tree's leaves in one verse** (WLC וּכְנֹבֶלֶת מִתְּאֵנָה, "as leaves falling from the fig tree"; lemma 8384 gated) `[T]`. Matthew has the stars falling (24:29) and, three verses later, the parable of the fig tree and its leaves (24:32) `[T]`. The same OT verse supplies both images `[I]`, moderate (Mark has the same sequence, Mark 13:25, 28). *What it adds:* the language of the end is the prophets' language for the fall of empires; the hearers who know Isaiah hear a day of the LORD on the powers `[I]`, high.

**Dan 7:13 + Zech 12:10–14 → 24:30** `[S: audit]` *(high for both)*. Accepted as the audit has it: ἐπὶ τῶν νεφελῶν τοῦ οὐρανοῦ ("on the clouds of heaven") is the OG's ἐπί ("on"; Theodotion μετά, "with") `[T]`; Zech 12:10–14 supplies κόψονται ("they will mourn") and "tribe by tribe"; Gen 12:3 / 28:14 πᾶσαι αἱ φυλαὶ τῆς γῆς ("all the tribes of the earth") is an overtone, low–moderate `[S: audit]`. Text-first extension: φυλή ("tribe") stands only at 19:28 and 24:30 in Matthew (SBLGNT) `[T]`, verified. At 19:28 the Twelve will judge "the twelve tribes of Israel"; at 24:30 "all the tribes of the earth" mourn `[T]`. νεφέλη ("cloud") stands at 17:5 (twice), 24:30 and 26:64 `[T]`, verified: the cloud of the transfiguration, the coming, and the claim at the trial `[I]`, moderate.

**Isa 27:13 → 24:31** *(moderate–high)*. *Source context:* "In that day a great trumpet will be blown, and those who were lost in the land of Assyria and those who were driven out to the land of Egypt will come and worship the LORD on the holy mountain at Jerusalem" (WLC בְּשׁוֹפָר גָּדוֹל; lemma 7782 gated) `[T]`. The verse before: "you will be gleaned one by one, O people of Israel" (27:12) `[T]`. *Text:* Swete and Rahlfs σαλπιοῦσιν τῇ σάλπιγγι τῇ μεγάλῃ ("they will blow the great trumpet") `[T]`. τῇ σάλπιγγι τῇ μεγάλῃ ("the great trumpet") stands in Swete only at Isa 27:13 `[T]`, verified; Rahlfs the same `[T]`. Matthew has σάλπιγγος μεγάλης ("a great trumpet"), without articles `[T]`. Mark lacks the trumpet `[T]`. *What it adds:* the trumpet of the return of the scattered to Zion; Matthew adds it to the gathering of the elect `[I]`. The overview has moderate; Matthew's addition against Mark and the unique Swete phrase argue for moderate–high.

**Zech 2:10 (Swete 2:6) → 24:31** *(moderate–high; Greek only)*. *Source context:* "Up! Up! Flee from the land of the north, declares the LORD" `[T]`. The Hebrew continues כִּי כְּאַרְבַּע רוּחוֹת הַשָּׁמַיִם פֵּרַשְׂתִּי אֶתְכֶם ("for I have spread you abroad as the four winds of the heavens", WLC; lemmas 6566, 7307 gated) `[T]`. Swete and Rahlfs: διότι ἐκ τῶν τεσσάρων ἀνέμων τοῦ οὐρανοῦ συνάξω ὑμᾶς ("for from the four winds of heaven I will gather you") `[T]`. **This is a substantive divergence**: the Hebrew says God *scattered* them as the four winds; the Greek says he will *gather* them from the four winds `[T]`. Matthew's "gather … from the four winds" (24:31) agrees with the Greek `[T]`. ἐκ τῶν τεσσάρων ἀνέμων ("from the four winds") stands in Swete only at Zech 2:6 `[T]`, verified (overview); Rahlfs the same `[T]`. Mark 13:27 has it too, so it is inherited `[T]`. *Nearest text:* the Greek `[T]`. *What it adds:* a gathering promise exists only in the Greek of this verse; Matthew's wording needs it `[I]`. The overview's row should record the divergence.

**Deut 30:4 → 24:31** *(moderate–high — new)*. *Source context:* after the curses and exile, return: "If your outcasts are in the end of the heavens (בִּקְצֵה הַשָּׁמָיִם), from there the LORD your God will gather you (יְקַבֶּצְךָ), and from there he will take you" (WLC; lemmas 7097, 6908 gated) `[T]`. The next verses promise the land, and the circumcision of the heart "so that you will love the LORD your God with all your heart and with all your soul" (30:6) `[T]`. *Text:* Swete and Rahlfs ἀπ' ἄκρου τοῦ οὐρανοῦ ἕως ἄκρου τοῦ οὐρανοῦ, ἐκεῖθεν συνάξει σε Κύριος ("from one end of heaven to the other end of heaven, from there the Lord will gather you") `[T]`. ἀπ' ἄκρου τοῦ οὐρανοῦ ("from the end of heaven") stands in Swete only at Deut 30:4; Neh 1:9 (which quotes Deuteronomy); Ps 18:7 (= 19:7, the sun's course) `[T]`, verified; Rahlfs the same three `[T]`. Matthew alters Mark's "from the end of the earth to the end of heaven" to ἀπ' ἄκρων οὐρανῶν ἕως τῶν ἄκρων αὐτῶν ("from the ends of heaven to their ends") `[T]`. That is the shape of Deut 30:4 Greek, heaven to heaven, with gathering in the same sentence `[I]`. *What it adds:* the gathering of the elect is the Torah's promised return from exile `[I]`, moderate–high. *OT-to-OT:* Deut 30:4, Isa 27:13 and Zech 2:10 are all promises of gathering the scattered; Matthew's verse joins all three `[I]`. Not in the overview or the audit.

**Gen 19:17 → 24:16–18** *(moderate — new)*. Move 1: the angels to Lot: "Escape for your life! Do not look behind you, and do not stop anywhere in the valley; escape to the hills" (WLC הָהָרָה הִמָּלֵט) `[T]`. Swete: μὴ περιβλέψῃς εἰς τὰ ὀπίσω … εἰς τὸ ὄρος σῴζου ("do not look behind … be saved to the mountain") `[T]`. Matthew: φευγέτωσαν ἐπὶ τὰ ὄρη ("let them flee to the mountains"), and "let him not turn back" (μὴ ἐπιστρεψάτω ὀπίσω, 24:18) `[T]`. Luke 17:29–32 names Lot and his wife in the parallel sayings `[T]`; Matthew does not `[T]`. The pattern (flee to the mountains; do not go back) is Gen 19 `[I]`, moderate. Matthew names Noah, not Lot, in the next unit (24:37) `[T]`.

**Dan 2:28 → 24:6** *(moderate)*. Move 1: δεῖ γενέσθαι ("must take place") stands in OG and Theodotion Dan 2:28–29, 45, of what God has revealed "in the latter days" `[T]`. Mark 13:7 has the same `[T]`. Move 1 only.

*Internal:* **Answers §5:1** — the seated teacher on a mountain, the disciples approaching (moderate–high). **Answers §5:17–18** — καταλύω ("to tear down, abolish") and "heaven and earth will pass away" (high, verified). **Answers §10:17–22** — "hated by all", "endures to the end" (high, verified; overview). **Answers §4:23; §9:35** — "the gospel of the kingdom" (high, verified). **Answers §2:1; §8:11** — ἀπὸ ἀνατολῶν ("from the east") (moderate, verified). **Answers §12:38–39; §16:1–4** — the sign from heaven (moderate). **Answers §7:15** — ψευδοπροφῆται ("false prophets": 7:15; 24:11, 24 only) (high, verified). **Answers §13:39–49** — συντέλεια τοῦ αἰῶνος ("end of the age") (high, verified). **Answers §21:19** — fig tree and leaves (moderate, verified). **Answers §22:14** — ἐκλεκτοί ("elect") (moderate–high, verified). **Answers §23:37–38** — ἐπισυνάγω ("to gather together"); the house desolate (high). **Answers §19:28** — φυλή ("tribe") (moderate, verified). **Plants §26:61; §27:40** — καταλύω ("to destroy") of the temple (high, verified). **Plants §26:64** — "coming on the clouds of heaven" (high; overview). **Plants §27:45, 51–54; §28:2–3** — darkness, earthquake, lightning (moderate; σεισμός ("earthquake") at 8:24; 24:7; 27:54; 28:2, overview; ἀστραπή ("lightning") 24:27; 28:3, verified). **Plants §28:20** — "the end of the age" (high, verified). **Plants §25:10** — θύρα ("door"): "near, at the doors" (24:33), then the door shut (25:10); θύρα stands at 6:6; 24:33; 25:10; 27:60 (moderate, verified). Opening material checked: 2:1 (from the east) and 1:1 (Abraham, under the "all the tribes of the earth" overtone) are answered here.

**Translations.** At 24:30 the NASB95 has "in the sky" and "the clouds of the sky"; the ESV has "in heaven" and "the clouds of heaven" `[T]`. At 26:64 the NASB95 has "CLOUDS OF HEAVEN" `[T]`. The NASB95 breaks the link between 24:30 and 26:64 and hides Dan 7:13 ("clouds of heaven"); the ESV keeps both. At 24:31 the NASB95 has "from one end of the sky to the other" and the ESV "from one end of heaven to the other" `[T]`; only the ESV lets Deut 30:4 be heard. At 24:31 the NASB95's "a great trumpet" keeps Isa 27:13; the ESV's "a loud trumpet call" loses it `[T]`. At 24:12 the ESV's "the love of many" keeps τῶν πολλῶν ("of the many"); the NASB95 has "most people's love" `[T]`. At 24:33 the NASB95 has "right at the door"; the ESV "at the very gates" `[T]`; the ESV loses the θύρα ("door") link to 25:10. **Pulpit divergence:** the ESV is the better pulpit text for 24:30–31 (heaven, Dan 7:13, Deut 30:4); the NASB95 is better for the trumpet and the door. The congregation hearing the ESV will hear "clouds of heaven" at 24:30 and 26:64 as one phrase.

**Difficulty.** *(1) "This generation" (24:34).* Options: (a) Jesus' contemporaries, with "all these things" the events up to the temple's fall `[S]`; (b) "this kind" of people, through history `[S]`; (c) a split discourse, with 24:4–35 the temple and 24:36–25:46 the end `[S]`. ἡ γενεὰ αὕτη ("this generation") and its oblique forms stand at 11:16; 12:41, 42, 45; 23:36; 24:34 (SBLGNT), always of Jesus' hearers `[T]`, verified; 23:36 has just said "all these things will come upon this generation" `[T]`. *(2) Is 24:29–31 the end of the world or the judgement on Jerusalem in cosmic language?* Options: (a) the final coming `[S]`; (b) the fall of the temple, told in the prophets' language for the fall of nations (Isa 13; 34) `[S]`; (c) both, the one a pattern of the other `[S]`. The text has "immediately after" (24:29) and the gathering of the elect from the whole earth (24:31) `[T]`. Present the options.

**Pitfall.** Reading 24 as a timetable: counting the signs to find the date. The discourse's first word is "see that no one misleads you" (24:4), and the signs named in 24:5–12 are said to be "not yet the end" (24:6) and "the beginning of birth pangs" (24:8) `[T]`. Its next unit says "of that day no one knows" (24:36) `[T]`. The corrective: preach the signs as warnings against being misled and as calls to endurance (24:13), and preach 24:35 as the anchor. See Trap 4.

**Book-overview note:** *Extends.* (1) Add **Deut 30:4 → 24:31** (moderate–high, new): Matthew changes Mark's "earth to heaven" to the Greek of Deut 30:4, "end of heaven to end of heaven", with gathering. (2) The **Zech 2:10** row should record that the Hebrew says "I have scattered you as the four winds" and the Greek "I will gather you from the four winds": a substantive divergence, and Matthew needs the Greek. (3) Add **Dan 12:1** (Theodotion οἵα οὐ γέγονεν, "such as has not been") → 24:21, inherited from Mark (moderate–high), and note that Dan 12:1–2 runs on to 25:46. (4) The **Isa 13:10; 34:4** row could note Isa 34:4's fig tree beside the falling stars (24:29, 32) and Joel 2:10 for φέγγος ("light"). (5) Raise **Isa 27:13** to moderate–high (unique Swete phrase; Matthew's addition to Mark). (6) Echo Table: add καταλύω ("abolish, tear down") 5:17 → 24:2 → 26:61; 27:40; ὁ οὐρανὸς καὶ ἡ γῆ ("heaven and earth") + παρέρχομαι ("to pass away") 5:18 → 24:35; εὐαγγέλιον τῆς βασιλείας ("gospel of the kingdom") 4:23; 9:35 → 24:14; ἀστραπή ("lightning") 24:27 → 28:3. (7) The English table: the NASB95's "sky" at 24:30–31 against its own "heaven" at 26:64.

---

## 46. Matthew 24:36–25:13 — Of That Day No One Knows; the Ten Virgins ⭐

**Position.** *Q1:* 24:4–35 gave signs, the coming of the Son of Man, and the fig tree's lesson: "this generation will not pass away until all these things take place" (24:34) `[T]`. *Q2:* this unit exists here because, after the signs, the discourse turns on its hinge: Περὶ δὲ τῆς ἡμέρας ἐκείνης ("but of that day", 24:36). The day itself cannot be calculated, so the rest of the discourse is about readiness, not timing `[T]`/`[I]`, high. It plants the command γρηγορεῖτε ("keep watch"), which the disciples will fail in Gethsemane (26:38–41) `[T]`.

**Structure.** No one knows the day (24:36); the days of Noah (24:37–41); the thief (24:42–44); the faithful and wicked slave (24:45–51); the ten virgins (25:1–13) `[T]`. **An inclusio:** γρηγορεῖτε οὖν, ὅτι οὐκ οἴδατε ("therefore keep watch, because you do not know", 24:42) and γρηγορεῖτε οὖν, ὅτι οὐκ οἴδατε (25:13) `[T]`.

**Text-first findings.**

- **"Nor the Son."** οὐδεὶς οἶδεν, οὐδὲ οἱ ἄγγελοι τῶν οὐρανῶν οὐδὲ ὁ υἱός, εἰ μὴ ὁ πατὴρ μόνος ("no one knows, not even the angels of heaven, nor the Son, but the Father alone", 24:36) `[T]`. NA28 prints οὐδὲ ὁ υἱός within sigla; the overview records it read by א*·²ᵇ B D Θ ƒ¹³ and omitted by the majority text `[S]` (overview §6). Both English versions print it `[T]`.
- **Noah: those "taken" are swept away.** ἕως ἦλθεν ὁ κατακλυσμὸς καὶ ἦρεν ἅπαντας ("until the flood came and took them all away", 24:39) `[T]`. κατακλυσμός ("flood") occurs only at 24:38, 39 and κιβωτός ("ark") only at 24:38 (SBLGNT) `[T]`, verified. In the analogy the people of Noah's day are the ones taken; then εἷς παραλαμβάνεται καὶ εἷς ἀφίεται ("one will be taken and one will be left", 24:40) `[T]`. The overview's Trap 4 rests here; confirmed. The verbs differ (αἴρω, "take away", 24:39; παραλαμβάνω, "take along", 24:40–41) `[T]`, so the identification of "taken" with judgement is `[I]`, moderate–high, not `[T]`.
- **Watch: six times, then Gethsemane.** γρηγορέω ("to keep watch") occurs at 24:42, 43; 25:13; 26:38, 40, 41 (SBLGNT) `[T]`, verified. **The command of the discourse is repeated in the garden, and the disciples sleep.** Verified; synthetic, high for the chain. The Mark sweep found the same pattern in Mark (13:34–37; 14:34–38), and Matthew keeps it `[S]`.
- **Wise and foolish: the Sermon's builders return.** φρόνιμος ("wise, prudent") occurs at 7:24; 10:16; 24:45; 25:2, 4, 8, 9 and μωρός ("foolish") at 5:22; 7:26; 23:17; 25:2, 3, 8 (SBLGNT) `[T]`, verified. The wise and foolish virgins are the wise and foolish builders of 7:24–27 `[I]`, high.
- **"I do not know you."** Ἀμὴν λέγω ὑμῖν, οὐκ οἶδα ὑμᾶς ("truly I say to you, I do not know you", 25:12). οὐκ οἶδα ("I do not know") stands at 25:12; 26:70, 72, 74 (SBLGNT phrase search) `[T]`, verified — the bridegroom's word to the foolish virgins, and Peter's three denials (οὐκ οἶδα τὸν ἄνθρωπον, "I do not know the man", 26:72, 74). *Synthetic*, moderate–high: Peter says of Jesus what the bridegroom says to those shut out. Compare 7:23 οὐδέποτε ἔγνων ὑμᾶς ("I never knew you"), with a different verb `[T]`.
- **The bridegroom.** νυμφίος ("bridegroom") occurs at 9:15 (twice); 25:1, 5, 6, 10 (SBLGNT) `[T]`, verified: "the days will come when the bridegroom is taken away" (9:15) and his delayed coming (25:5) `[T]`. παρθένος ("virgin") occurs only at 1:23 and 25:1, 7, 11 `[T]`, verified.
- **Weeping and gnashing of teeth.** ὁ κλαυθμὸς καὶ ὁ βρυγμὸς τῶν ὀδόντων at 8:12; 13:42, 50; 22:13; 24:51; 25:30 (SBLGNT phrase search) `[T]`, verified: six times, a Matthean refrain (Luke has it once, 13:28) `[I]`. κλαυθμός ("weeping") also stands at 2:18, Rachel weeping `[T]`.

**Synoptic check.** Mark 13:32–37 has "nor the Son" and a short watch parable. Matthew adds the Noah analogy and the slave parable (double tradition, Luke 17:26–27, 34–35; 12:39–46), and the ten virgins (Matthew alone) `[T]`.

**Tool 11.** **Gen 6–7 → 24:37–39** *(high)*. *Source context:* the flood; Noah enters the ark (Swete Gen 7:7 εἰς τὴν κιβωτὸν διὰ τὸ ὕδωρ τοῦ κατακλυσμοῦ, "into the ark because of the water of the flood") `[T]`. *What it adds:* the people of Noah's day were not wicked in the analogy so much as unaware — eating, drinking, marrying (24:38). The judgement came on ordinary life `[I]`, high. Confirms the overview's row.

*Internal:* **answers §7:24–27** — wise and foolish (high, verified). **Answers §9:15** — the bridegroom (high, verified). **Answers §1:23** — παρθένος (verbal only; uncertain as design). **Plants §26:38–41** — γρηγορέω (high, verified). **Plants §26:70–74** — οὐκ οἶδα (moderate–high, verified). **Plants §25:30** — the refrain (high, verified).

**Translations.** NASB95 and ESV both "keep watch" / "watch" and both print "nor the Son" `[T]`. **Pulpit divergence:** none bearing.

**Pitfall.** Reading 24:40–41 as a rapture text. In the controlling analogy the flood "took them all away" (24:39), and the parable that follows ends with a door shut (25:10). The corrective: read 24:40–41 by 24:37–39, as the overview's Trap 4 says, and preach watchfulness.

**Book-overview note:** confirms Trap 4 and the Gen 6–7 row. Extends with three verified chains for the Echo Table: γρηγορέω (24:42 → 26:41), φρόνιμος/μωρός (7:24–26 ↔ 25:2–9), and οὐκ οἶδα (25:12 ↔ 26:70–74).

---

## 47. Matthew 25:14–30 — The Talents

**Position.** *Q1:* the ten virgins ended "keep watch" (25:13) `[T]`. *Q2:* this unit exists here because watching is now defined as work: the master goes away, entrusts his property, and returns "after a long time" (μετὰ δὲ πολὺν χρόνον, 25:19) to settle accounts `[T]`. It stands between readiness (25:1–13) and judgement by deeds (25:31–46) `[I]`, high.

**Structure.** The entrusting (25:14–15); the three slaves at work or not (25:16–18); the reckoning: two commended in the same words, the third condemned (25:19–30) `[T]`. The commendations are verbatim: Εὖ, δοῦλε ἀγαθὲ καὶ πιστέ … εἴσελθε εἰς τὴν χαρὰν τοῦ κυρίου σου ("well done, good and faithful slave … enter into the joy of your master", 25:21, 23) `[T]`.

**Text-first findings.**

- **Faithful slave, wicked slave.** δοῦλε ἀγαθὲ καὶ πιστέ ("good and faithful slave", 25:21, 23) against Πονηρὲ δοῦλε καὶ ὀκνηρέ ("you wicked, lazy slave", 25:26) `[T]`. πιστός ("faithful") occurs only at 24:45; 25:21 (twice), 23 (twice) (SBLGNT) `[T]`, verified: the faithful slave of 24:45 is the faithful slave here.
- **The master's joy.** εἴσελθε εἰς τὴν χαρὰν τοῦ κυρίου σου (25:21, 23). χαρά ("joy") stands at 2:10; 13:20, 44; 25:21, 23; 28:8 (SBLGNT) `[T]`, verified: the magi's joy (2:10), the man who sells all "from joy" for the treasure (13:44), and the women running from the tomb "with great joy" (28:8) `[T]`.
- **The buried talent.** ἔκρυψα τὸ τάλαντόν σου ἐν τῇ γῇ ("I hid your talent in the ground", 25:25). κρύπτω ("to hide") occurs at 5:14; 11:25; 13:35, 44 (twice); 25:18, 25 `[T]`, verified. The treasure hidden in a field is found and bought with joy (13:44); the talent hidden in the ground is lost in fear (φοβηθείς, "being afraid", 25:25) `[I]`, moderate–high.
- **The servant's picture of his master.** ἔγνων σε ὅτι σκληρὸς εἶ ἄνθρωπος ("I knew you to be a hard man", 25:24) `[T]`. The master repeats the words without accepting the description (25:26–27) `[T]`. The parable shows that what the slave believed about his master governed what he did `[I]`, high.
- **Outer darkness.** εἰς τὸ σκότος τὸ ἐξώτερον ("into the outer darkness", 25:30), with the refrain of weeping and gnashing (8:12; 22:13; 25:30 have the outer darkness; the refrain stands six times) `[T]`, verified.

**Synoptic check.** Luke 19:11–27 has a related parable of minas, with a nobleman, ten slaves and a rebellion. The wording overlaps at the verdict (Luke 19:22–26). Matthew's parable — talents, three slaves, "the joy of your master", the outer darkness — is otherwise his own `[T]`. Not in Mark.

**Tool 11.** No OT citation. *Move 1 note:* τάλαντον ("talent") last appeared at 18:24, the debt of ten thousand talents forgiven `[T]`, verified. *Synthetic*, uncertain: what was forgiven there is entrusted here.

*Internal:* **answers §24:45–51** — the faithful slave (high, verified). **Answers §13:44** — hidden treasure and joy (moderate–high, verified). **Answers §18:24** — talents (uncertain as design). **Plants §28:8** — joy (moderate, verified). **Plants §25:31–46** — the reckoning (high).

**Translations.** τάλαντον: both NASB95 and ESV "talent(s)". The English word "talent" has come to mean natural ability, from this parable; the Greek is a sum of money `[T]`. **Pulpit divergence:** none between versions; but the congregation hears "talents" as gifts. Say what a talent was.

**Pitfall.** Preaching "use your talents" as a lesson in gifts and self-improvement. The parable is about the returning master, faithfulness while he is away, and the slave whose fear came from a false view of his master. The corrective: preach it within 24:36–25:46, where every parable asks whether the servant is ready for the Lord's return.

**Book-overview note:** confirms the unit's place in the fifth discourse. Extends: πιστός (24:45 ↔ 25:21–23) and χαρά (2:10 → 28:8) for the Echo Table.

---

## 48. Matthew 25:31–46 — The Sheep and the Goats ⭐

**Position.** *Q1:* the fifth discourse has asked, three times in parables, whether the servants are ready for the master's return (24:45–25:30) `[T]`. *Q2:* this unit exists here because the discourse ends by showing the return itself: the Son of Man on his throne, all the nations gathered, and the separation. It is the last teaching before the passion (26:1–2), and the King who judges is the one handed over to be crucified two verses later `[T]`/`[I]`, high.

**Structure.** The Son of Man enthroned and the nations gathered (25:31–33); the King's word to those on the right (25:34–40); to those on the left (25:41–45); the verdict (25:46) `[T]`. The list of six needs is given four times, each time shorter: hungry, thirsty, stranger, naked, sick, in prison (25:35–36, 37–39, 42–43, 44) `[T]`.

**Text-first findings.**

- **Son of Man, Shepherd and King.** ὁ υἱὸς τοῦ ἀνθρώπου ἐν τῇ δόξῃ αὐτοῦ ("the Son of Man … in His glory", 25:31) becomes ὁ ποιμήν ("the shepherd", 25:32) and then ὁ βασιλεύς ("the King", 25:34, 40) `[T]`. ἐπὶ θρόνου δόξης αὐτοῦ ("on His glorious throne") occurs only at 19:28 and 25:31 (SBLGNT) `[T]`, verified. ποιμήν ("shepherd") occurs only at 9:36; 25:32; 26:31 `[T]`, verified: the sheep without a shepherd, the judging shepherd, the shepherd struck.
- **"All the nations."** συναχθήσονται ἔμπροσθεν αὐτοῦ πάντα τὰ ἔθνη ("all the nations will be gathered before Him", 25:32). πάντα τὰ ἔθνη ("all the nations") stands in this case only at 25:32 and 28:19 (SBLGNT phrase search), and "all the nations" in other cases at 24:9 (ὑπὸ πάντων τῶν ἐθνῶν) and 24:14 (πᾶσιν τοῖς ἔθνεσιν) `[T]`, verified. The nations to whom the gospel is preached (24:14) and who are to be discipled (28:19) are the nations gathered for judgement `[T]`/`[I]`, high.
- **Right and left.** τοῖς ἐκ δεξιῶν αὐτοῦ … τοῖς ἐξ εὐωνύμων ("to those on His right … to those on His left", 25:34, 41). See unit 40: ἐξ εὐωνύμων stands only at 20:21, 23; 25:33, 41; 27:38 `[T]`, verified. The King's right and left here, the robbers' places at the cross there `[I]`, moderate–high.
- **"The least of these my brothers."** ἑνὶ τούτων τῶν ἀδελφῶν μου τῶν ἐλαχίστων ("one of these brothers of Mine, even the least of them", 25:40). ἐλάχιστος ("least") occurs at 2:6; 5:19 (twice); 25:40, 45 (SBLGNT) `[T]`, verified. "My brothers" in Jesus' mouth: those who do the Father's will (12:49–50) and, after the resurrection, the disciples (28:10) `[T]`. The identity of "the least" is the unit's crux; see *Difficulty*.
- **Prepared kingdom, prepared fire.** κληρονομήσατε τὴν ἡτοιμασμένην ὑμῖν βασιλείαν ἀπὸ καταβολῆς κόσμου ("inherit the kingdom prepared for you from the foundation of the world", 25:34) against τὸ πῦρ τὸ αἰώνιον τὸ ἡτοιμασμένον τῷ διαβόλῳ καὶ τοῖς ἀγγέλοις αὐτοῦ ("the eternal fire which has been prepared for the devil and his angels", 25:41) `[T]`. The kingdom is prepared for people; the fire is prepared for the devil `[T]`. ἀπὸ καταβολῆς ("from the foundation") stands only at 13:35 and 25:34 `[T]`, verified. διάβολος ("devil") stands at 4:1, 5, 8, 11; 13:39; 25:41 `[T]`, verified: the tempter of 4:1–11 last appears here, under sentence.
- **The stranger.** ξένος ("stranger") occurs at 25:35, 38, 43, 44 and once more, at 27:7: the potter's field bought with the blood-money εἰς ταφὴν τοῖς ξένοις ("as a burial place for strangers") `[T]`, verified. *Synthetic*, moderate.
- **The verdict.** εἰς κόλασιν αἰώνιον, οἱ δὲ δίκαιοι εἰς ζωὴν αἰώνιον ("into eternal punishment, but the righteous into eternal life", 25:46) `[T]`. κόλασις ("punishment") occurs only here `[T]`. ζωὴν αἰώνιον ("eternal life") closes the young man's question (19:16, 29) `[T]`, verified.

**Synoptic check.** The unit is Matthew's alone. Mark and Luke end their discourses with watchfulness (Mark 13:33–37; Luke 21:34–36) `[T]`.

**Tool 11.** **Dan 12:2 → 25:46** *(high)*. *Source context:* the resurrection at the end: many who sleep in the dust will awake, some to everlasting life, some to everlasting contempt. Swete εἰς ζωὴν αἰώνιον ("to eternal life") `[T]`. The phrase occurs in canonical Swete only at Dan 12:2 (overview, gated) `[T]`. Matthew pairs it with κόλασιν αἰώνιον ("eternal punishment"), not Daniel's αἰσχύνην αἰώνιον ("eternal shame") `[T]`. Confirms the overview's row.

**Ezek 34:17 → 25:32–33** *(moderate)*. *Source context:* the LORD as shepherd judges between sheep and sheep, between rams and goats (Swete κριῶν καὶ τράγων, "rams and he-goats"), after condemning Israel's shepherds; then he sets "my servant David" over them (34:23) `[T]`. Matthew's ἔριφος ("goat") is not Ezekiel's word `[T]`; the link is the shepherd-judge, not the vocabulary `[I]`, moderate. Confirms the overview's row.

**Zech 14:5 → 25:31** *(moderate)*. Swete ἥξει Κύριος ὁ θεός μου, καὶ πάντες οἱ ἅγιοι μετ' αὐτοῦ ("the LORD my God will come, and all the holy ones with Him") against Matthew πάντες οἱ ἄγγελοι μετ' αὐτοῦ ("all the angels with Him") `[T]`. The Son of Man comes as the LORD comes `[I]`, moderate–high. Confirms the overview's row.

*Internal:* **answers §19:28** — the throne of glory (high, verified). **Answers §9:36** — the shepherd (high, verified). **Answers §12:49–50** — "my brothers" (high). **Answers §4:1–11** — the devil (high, verified). **Answers §10:40–42** — receiving the little ones and the cup of cold water (moderate–high). **Plants §26:31** — the shepherd struck (high, verified). **Plants §27:7, 38** — strangers; right and left (moderate, verified). **Plants §28:19** — all the nations (high, verified).

**Translations.** 25:40 ἀδελφῶν μου τῶν ἐλαχίστων: NASB95 "these brothers of Mine, even the least of them"; ESV "the least of these my brothers" (with a note "or brothers and sisters") `[S]`. **Pulpit divergence:** none bearing on the crux.

**Difficulty.** Who are "the least of these my brothers"? *Function in context:* the end of a discourse addressed to disciples (24:3), in which they will be hated, handed over and imprisoned (24:9) and must preach to all the nations (24:14) `[T]`. Options: (a) any person in need; (b) Jesus' disciples, especially those sent (10:40–42, where receiving "one of these little ones" is receiving Jesus); (c) Christians in need `[S]`. "My brothers" in Matthew means disciples (12:49–50; 28:10) and the "little ones" are disciples (10:42; 18:6, 10, 14) `[T]`, which favours (b) or (c) `[I]`, moderate–high. The overview's Trap 7 handles this; confirmed.

**Pitfall.** Preaching works as the basis of acceptance. The kingdom was "prepared … from the foundation of the world" (25:34), and the righteous are surprised by their own deeds (25:37–39). The corrective: preach the King's identification with his brothers, and the deeds as the fruit by which the tree is known (7:16–20).

**Book-overview note:** confirms the Dan 12:2, Ezek 34 and Zech 14:5 rows and Trap 7. Extends with verified chains: ποιμήν (9:36 → 25:32 → 26:31), πάντα τὰ ἔθνη (25:32 ↔ 28:19; cf. 24:9, 14), διάβολος (4:1–11 → 25:41), ξένος (25:35–44 ↔ 27:7).

---

## 49. Matthew 26:1–16 — Anointed for Burial; Thirty Pieces of Silver

**Position.** *Q1:* the fifth discourse has ended on the sheep and the goats, "eternal punishment … eternal life" (25:46), and the Son of Man has been shown on his throne of glory (25:31). *Q2:* this unit exists here because the judge of all the nations must now be handed over. The teaching is closed for good (26:1), and Jesus himself names the day and the cross (26:2). Three scenes then set the passion moving: a plot, an anointing, a price. The one who will judge "the least of these" (25:40, 45) is anointed by a woman and sold by one of the Twelve `[T]`/`[I]`.

**Structure.** The formula and the fourth passion announcement (1–2); the plot in Caiaphas' court (3–5); the anointing at Bethany (6–13); Judas and the chief priests (14–16). The anointing is set between the plot and the bargain: the sandwich is Mark's (Mark 14:1–11), and Matthew keeps it `[T]`. Two verbs frame the unit: παραδίδοται ("is handed over", 26:2) and ἵνα αὐτὸν παραδῷ ("that he might hand him over", 26:16) `[T]`.

**Text-first findings.**

- **"All" the words: the teaching is finished.** Καὶ ἐγένετο ὅτε ἐτέλεσεν ὁ Ἰησοῦς πάντας τοὺς λόγους τούτους ("and it happened, when Jesus had finished all these words", 26:1). ἐτέλεσεν ὁ Ἰησοῦς ("Jesus had finished") stands at 7:28; 11:1; 13:53; 19:1; 26:1 (SBLGNT), and πάντας τοὺς λόγους ("all the words") only at 26:1 `[T]`, verified. The next words Jesus speaks are not teaching but a date and a death. The Deuteronomy frame (Deut 31:1 Swete and Rahlfs; 32:45 Hebrew) stands as audited `[S: audit]`; see Tool 11.
- **Jesus announces the date himself.** Οἴδατε ὅτι μετὰ δύο ἡμέρας τὸ πάσχα γίνεται, καὶ ὁ υἱὸς τοῦ ἀνθρώπου παραδίδοται εἰς τὸ σταυρωθῆναι ("you know that after two days the Passover is coming, and the Son of Man is handed over to be crucified", 26:2). In Mark the narrator gives the date (Mark 14:1); in Matthew it is the fourth passion saying, after 16:21; 17:22–23; 20:18–19 `[T]`. παραδίδοται ("is handed over") is present tense, and the form stands at 26:2, 24, 45 only (SBLGNT) `[T]`, verified. The plot of 26:3–5 follows the announcement: Jesus knows before his enemies meet `[I]`, high.
- **The plot is gathered, and fears a riot.** τότε συνήχθησαν οἱ ἀρχιερεῖς καὶ οἱ πρεσβύτεροι τοῦ λαοῦ ("then the chief priests and the elders of the people were gathered together", 26:3). They plan to take him δόλῳ ("by stealth", 26:4; δόλος ("stealth") stands only here in Matthew) and not during the feast, ἵνα μὴ θόρυβος γένηται ἐν τῷ λαῷ ("otherwise a riot might occur among the people", 26:5) `[T]`. θόρυβος ("riot") occurs at 26:5 and 27:24 only (SBLGNT) `[T]`, verified. At 27:24 the riot they feared is exactly what drives Pilate to wash his hands. Their own caution is overruled, and the death falls at the feast after all `[I]`, moderate–high.
- **The woman's act becomes part of the gospel.** She pours μύρον ("perfume", 26:7, 12) on his head, and Jesus reads the act: πρὸς τὸ ἐνταφιάσαι με ἐποίησεν ("she did it to prepare me for burial", 26:12). ἐνταφιάζω ("to prepare for burial") occurs once in Matthew, and in the New Testament elsewhere only at John 19:40 `[T]`, verified. Then: ὅπου ἐὰν κηρυχθῇ τὸ εὐαγγέλιον τοῦτο ἐν ὅλῳ τῷ κόσμῳ ("wherever this gospel is preached in the whole world", 26:13). εὐαγγέλιον ("gospel") stands four times in Matthew: 4:23; 9:35; 24:14; 26:13 (SBLGNT) `[T]`, verified. The last two are both "preached … in the whole world": first as "this gospel of the kingdom" (24:14), then as "this gospel" that includes her act. **The good news that will reach the nations has a burial in it, and a woman’s memorial in it** `[I]`, moderate–high. μνημόσυνον ("memorial") stands only here in Matthew `[T]`, verified.
- **A good work, not waste.** The disciples call it ἀπώλεια ("waste", 26:8). Jesus calls it ἔργον … καλόν ("a good deed", 26:10), the phrase of 5:16, "that they may see your good works" (τὰ καλὰ ἔργα) `[T]`. The poor are not dismissed: πτωχός ("poor") runs 5:3; 11:5; 19:21; 26:9, 11 (SBLGNT, verified), and 19:21 had told the rich man to give to them `[T]`. The saying sets the poor beside a unique hour, not against it: ἐμὲ δὲ οὐ πάντοτε ἔχετε ("but you do not always have me", 26:11). Compare 28:20, πάσας τὰς ἡμέρας ("always", literally "all the days") `[T]` for the wording; *synthetic*, moderate.
- **Judas sets the price; the priests weigh it.** Τί θέλετέ μοι δοῦναι κἀγὼ ὑμῖν παραδώσω αὐτόν; ("what are you willing to give me, and I will hand him over to you?", 26:15). οἱ δὲ ἔστησαν αὐτῷ τριάκοντα ἀργύρια ("and they weighed out to him thirty pieces of silver") `[T]`. Mark has only a promise of "silver" (Mark 14:11). Matthew adds the sum and the verb ἵστημι ("to weigh out"), and both are Zech 11:12 (see Tool 11). ἀργύριον ("silver") stands at 25:18, 27 (the parable) and then only in the passion and the resurrection: 26:15; 27:3, 5, 6, 9; 28:12, 15 (SBLGNT) `[T]`, verified.
- **"From then on": the third ἀπὸ τότε.** καὶ ἀπὸ τότε ἐζήτει εὐκαιρίαν ἵνα αὐτὸν παραδῷ ("and from then on he began looking for a good opportunity to hand him over", 26:16). ἀπὸ τότε ("from then on") stands at 4:17; 16:21; 26:16 only (SBLGNT) `[T]`, verified. At 4:17 Jesus begins to preach; at 16:21 he begins to show that he must suffer; at 26:16 Judas begins to seek his chance. **The book's two hinges are matched by a third, and it belongs to the betrayer.** *Synthetic, verified*: high for the chain; `[I]` moderate–high that the third is meant as a hinge.

**Synoptic check.** Matthew follows Mark 14:1–11 closely. He changes four things. (1) The date becomes Jesus' own saying (26:2; compare Mark 14:1). (2) He names Καϊάφας ("Caiaphas", 26:3; again 26:57 only), whom Mark never names. (3) He drops Mark's "nard" and "three hundred denarii" and the broken flask, and makes the objectors οἱ μαθηταί ("the disciples", 26:8) where Mark has "some" (Mark 14:4). (4) He turns Mark's promise of silver into the weighing of thirty pieces (26:15). Mark's προέλαβεν μυρίσαι … εἰς τὸν ἐνταφιασμόν ("she has anointed beforehand … for burial", Mark 14:8) becomes πρὸς τὸ ἐνταφιάσαι με ("to prepare me for burial"). One consequence runs to the end of the book: in Matthew the women come to the tomb θεωρῆσαι ("to look at", 28:1), not to anoint (Mark 16:1). **This is the only anointing of his body in Matthew** `[T]` for the distribution; `[I]` for the design, moderate.

**Tool 11.**

**Deut 31:1 / 32:45 → 26:1** *(moderate–high)* `[S: audit]`. Accepted as audited. *Source context:* Moses finishes his words, and his death is announced at once (Deut 31:2, 14). *Book usage:* the fifth and last formula. *What it adds:* the teacher on the mountain (5:1) finishes as Moses finished, and in both a death follows. Text-first extension: the death is named in the very next verse in both books (Deut 31:2; Matt 26:2) `[T]`.

**Zech 11:12 → 26:15** *(high)*. *Source context:* the prophet, commissioned to shepherd "the flock doomed to slaughter" (11:4), breaks his staff Favour, and asks for his wage. They weigh out thirty pieces of silver: WLC וַיִּשְׁקְלוּ אֶת־שְׂכָרִי שְׁלֹשִׁים כָּסֶף ("and they weighed out my wage, thirty of silver"); Swete and Rahlfs alike, καὶ ἔστησαν τὸν μισθόν μου τριάκοντα ἀργυροῦς ("and they weighed out my wage, thirty silver pieces") `[T]`. The LORD calls it sarcastically "the lordly price at which I was valued by them" (11:13). *Book usage:* Zechariah is live (21:5 = Zech 9:9; 23:35; 24:30; 26:31). *OT-to-OT:* Exod 21:32 sets thirty shekels as the price of a gored slave *(moderate, `[I]`)*. *What it adds:* the price of Jesus is the price set on the LORD's own shepherd, and the valuers are the people's leaders. **Matthew stands with both texts:** ἔστησαν ("weighed out") is the Greek of both, and the Hebrew verb שָׁקַל ("to weigh") means the same. There is no divergence here; the divergence comes at 27:9–10 (see unit 53).

**Gen 37:26–28 → 26:14–16** *(uncertain–moderate; new candidate)*. Move 1 only. Ἰούδας ("Judah") proposes to his brothers that they sell Joseph rather than kill him, "and hide his blood" (Gen 37:26), and Joseph is sold. The Hebrew price is בְּעֶשְׂרִים כָּסֶף ("for twenty of silver", Gen 37:28); Swete has εἴκοσι χρυσῶν ("twenty gold pieces") `[T]`. Genesis 37 is already live in Matthew (Gen 37:20 at 21:38 `[S: audit]`). The name, the sale of a brother and the silver (in the Hebrew) are shared; no wording is. A name hyperlink, not a citation.

*Internal:* **Answers §4:17; §16:21** — ἀπὸ τότε ("from then on"), the third and last (high, verified). **Plants §27:24** — θόρυβος ("riot") (moderate–high, verified). **Plants §27:3–10** — the thirty pieces (high, verified). **Plants §28:1** — no second anointing (moderate). **Answers §24:14** — "this gospel … in the whole world" (moderate–high, verified). **Answers §5:16** — the "good work" (moderate). **Answers §19:21** — the poor (moderate). **Plants §28:20** — "you do not always have me" against "I am with you always" (moderate, synthetic). Opening chapters checked: Καϊάφας ("Caiaphas") and the gathering of chief priests and elders answer 2:4, where Herod "gathered all the chief priests and scribes of the people" (see the overview's Echo Table) `[T]`.

**Translations.** At 26:15 the NASB95 has "they **weighed out** thirty pieces of silver"; the ESV has "they **paid him** thirty pieces of silver". At 26:16 the NASB95 has "From then on" and the ESV "from that moment"; both render 4:17 and 16:21 as "From that time", so both hide the third ἀπὸ τότε ("from then on"). At 26:5 and 27:24 the NASB95 has "riot" twice; the ESV has "uproar" and then "riot". **Pulpit divergence:** the ESV's "paid" loses Zechariah's "weighed" (Zech 11:12, Hebrew and Greek alike). The congregation will not hear the prophet's verb in 26:15, so the link has to be made from the pulpit when 27:9–10 is reached.

**Pitfall.** "Mary's extravagant devotion — give Jesus your best." The unit does not name the woman, and Jesus' reading of her act is about his burial (26:12) and about the gospel (26:13), not about the size of the gift. The corrective: preach the anointing as the first public sign that the King is going to his grave, set between the plot and the price, and let her memorial be the gospel that tells of his death.

**Book-overview note:** **confirms with something new.** The overview names ἀπὸ τότε ("from then on") at 26:16 as an addendum to the two hinges. The unit shows it is a structural third: the betrayer's beginning stands in the same words as the preaching and the passion. **Extends:** θόρυβος ("riot") 26:5 → 27:24 is not in the Echo Table, and nor is 24:14 → 26:13 (εὐαγγέλιον, "gospel", preached in the whole world). The Synoptic point that Matthew has no anointing at the tomb (28:1 against Mark 16:1) gives 26:12 more weight than the overview states.

---

## 50. Matthew 26:17–35 — The Blood of the Covenant for the Forgiveness of Sins ⭐

**Position.** *Q1:* the King has been anointed for burial and priced at thirty pieces of silver (26:1–16). Jesus has named the day himself: "after two days the Passover" (26:2). *Q2:* this unit exists here because the death now has to be *interpreted* before it happens. The one who will be handed over gives his body and blood at the Passover table, and says what his death is for. The book's first promise, σώσει τὸν λαὸν αὐτοῦ ἀπὸ τῶν ἁμαρτιῶν αὐτῶν ("He will save His people from their sins", 1:21), is explained at this table and nowhere earlier `[T]`/`[I]`. Then the unit turns at once to the cost for the disciples: all of them will stumble, and Peter will deny him (26:31–35). The table is set between a betrayer and a denier `[T]`.

**Structure.** Preparation (17–19); the announcement of the betrayer (20–25); the bread and the cup (26–29); the walk to the Mount of Olives, the shepherd saying, and Peter's protest (30–35) `[T]`. Two "Truly I say to you" sayings frame the meal and the walk: εἷς ἐξ ὑμῶν παραδώσει με ("one of you will betray Me", 26:21) and τρὶς ἀπαρνήσῃ με ("you will deny Me three times", 26:34) `[T]`. The betrayal frames the table; the denial follows it.

**Text-first findings.**

- **The forgiveness of sins is named here, and only here.** τοῦτο γάρ ἐστιν τὸ αἷμά μου τῆς διαθήκης τὸ περὶ πολλῶν ἐκχυννόμενον εἰς ἄφεσιν ἁμαρτιῶν ("for this is My blood of the covenant, which is poured out for many for forgiveness of sins", 26:28). ἄφεσις ("forgiveness") stands once in Matthew (SBLGNT), and the phrase εἰς ἄφεσιν ἁμαρτιῶν ("for forgiveness of sins") once `[T]`, verified. Mark has the same phrase of John's baptism (Mark 1:4), and Luke likewise (Luke 3:3); Matthew's John baptises εἰς μετάνοιαν ("for repentance", 3:11), and the people only confess τὰς ἁμαρτίας αὐτῶν ("their sins", 3:6) `[T]`, verified. **Matthew has moved "for the forgiveness of sins" from the Jordan to the cup.** `[T]` for the distribution; `[I]` for the design, high. ἁμαρτία ("sin") runs 1:21; 3:6; 9:2, 5, 6; 12:31; 26:28 (SBLGNT) `[T]`, verified: the promise of 1:21 and the authority of 9:6 come to rest in a cup.
- **"Poured out": the righteous blood and his blood.** ἐκχυννόμενον ("poured out") stands in Matthew at 23:35 and 26:28 only (SBLGNT, phrase) `[T]`, verified. At 23:35 it is πᾶν αἷμα δίκαιον ἐκχυννόμενον ἐπὶ τῆς γῆς ("all the righteous blood shed on earth"), which will come "upon this generation". At 26:28 the same participle is his own blood, poured out "for many for forgiveness". *Synthetic, verified for the chain:* the blood that brings judgement (23:35) and the blood that brings forgiveness (26:28) are one word apart, and 27:25 brings them together (see unit 53) `[I]`, moderate–high.
- **"Drink from it, all of you."** Mark narrates ἔπιον ἐξ αὐτοῦ πάντες ("they all drank from it", Mark 14:23). Matthew makes it a command: Πίετε ἐξ αὐτοῦ πάντες ("Drink from it, all of you", 26:27), and adds φάγετε ("eat") to the bread (26:26) `[T]`. Then πάντες ("all") runs through the unit and beyond: Πάντες ὑμεῖς σκανδαλισθήσεσθε ("You will all fall away", 26:31); ὁμοίως καὶ πάντες οἱ μαθηταὶ εἶπαν ("all the disciples said the same thing too", 26:35); οἱ μαθηταὶ πάντες ἀφέντες αὐτὸν ἔφυγον ("all the disciples left Him and fled", 26:56) `[T]`, verified (phrases). **Every one who will flee is given the cup first** `[I]`, high.
- **The table answers the feedings.** λαβὼν … εὐλογήσας ἔκλασεν καὶ δοὺς τοῖς μαθηταῖς ("took … blessed … broke … gave it to the disciples", 26:26) repeats the words of 14:19 (εὐλογήσας κλάσας ἔδωκεν τοῖς μαθηταῖς), and εὐχαριστήσας ("when He had given thanks", 26:27) is the verb of 15:36. κλάω ("to break") stands at 14:19; 15:36; 26:26 only, and εὐχαριστέω ("to give thanks") at 15:36; 26:27 only (SBLGNT) `[T]`, verified. The bread of the crowds becomes the body given to the Twelve `[I]`, moderate–high.
- **"From now on": the three partings.** οὐ μὴ πίω ἀπ' ἄρτι ("I will not drink … from now on", 26:29) is Matthew's addition to Mark 14:25 (οὐκέτι, "no longer"). ἀπ' ἄρτι ("from now on") stands at 23:39; 26:29; 26:64 only (SBLGNT) `[T]`, verified. To Jerusalem: "you will not see Me until…" (23:39). To the disciples: "I will not drink … until" (26:29). To the high priest: "you will see the Son of Man" (26:64). Each is a parting with a promise in it `[I]`, moderate–high. And the new wine is drunk μεθ' ὑμῶν ("with you", 26:29): the phrase stands at 17:17; 26:29; 28:20, with μεθ' ἡμῶν ("with us") at 1:23 (see the overview's Echo Table) `[T]`, verified.
- **Judas calls him "Rabbi"; the others say "Lord".** Μήτι ἐγώ εἰμι, κύριε; ("Surely not I, Lord?", 26:22) against Μήτι ἐγώ εἰμι, ῥαββί; ("Surely it is not I, Rabbi?", 26:25) `[T]`. ῥαββί ("Rabbi") stands at 23:7, 8; 26:25, 49 only (SBLGNT): Jesus forbids the title to his disciples (23:8), and only Judas uses it of him, here and at the kiss `[T]`, verified. Jesus' reply Σὺ εἶπας ("You have said it yourself", 26:25) is Matthew's own, and it returns to the high priest at 26:64 (phrase at 26:25, 64 only; Σὺ λέγεις ("It is as you say") to Pilate, 27:11) `[T]`, verified.
- **Woe to the man.** οὐαὶ δὲ τῷ ἀνθρώπῳ ἐκείνῳ δι' οὗ ὁ υἱὸς τοῦ ἀνθρώπου παραδίδοται ("woe to that man by whom the Son of Man is betrayed", 26:24). The only other οὐαὶ τῷ ἀνθρώπῳ … δι' οὗ ("woe to that man through whom") is 18:7, of the one through whom the σκάνδαλον ("stumbling block") comes (SBLGNT, phrase) `[T]`, verified. The community discourse's warning about causing a little one to stumble is spoken here of the betrayer; and in the next breath all the disciples will "stumble" (σκανδαλισθήσεσθε, "fall away", 26:31) `[I]`, moderate.
- **Obedience in the same verb.** ἐποίησαν οἱ μαθηταὶ ὡς συνέταξεν αὐτοῖς ὁ Ἰησοῦς ("the disciples did as Jesus had directed them", 26:19) repeats 21:6 (καθὼς συνέταξεν αὐτοῖς ὁ Ἰησοῦς, "just as Jesus had instructed them"), the fetching of the donkey. συντάσσω ("to direct") stands at 21:6; 26:19; 27:10 only (SBLGNT) `[T]`, verified. The third is the formula's καθὰ συνέταξέν μοι κύριος ("as the Lord directed me", 27:10). Jesus' instructions and the Lord's stand in one verb `[I]`, moderate.
- **"My time is near."** Ὁ καιρός μου ἐγγύς ἐστιν ("My time is near", 26:18) is Matthew's own (compare Mark 14:14). ἐγγύς ("near") stands at 24:32, 33; 26:18 only (SBLGNT) `[T]`, verified: summer is near, "He is near, right at the door" (24:33), and now "My time is near" `[I]`, moderate.

**Synoptic check.** Matthew follows Mark 14:12–31 closely and changes it at the points that carry weight. (1) He drops Mark's man with the water jar and adds Ὁ καιρός μου ἐγγύς ἐστιν ("My time is near", 26:18). (2) He drops Mark's ὁ ἐσθίων μετ' ἐμοῦ ("one who is eating with Me", Mark 14:18), which echoes Ps 41:10 (Swete 40:10, ὁ ἐσθίων ἄρτους μου, "who ate my bread") `[T]`. (3) He adds Judas' own question and the reply Σὺ εἶπας ("You have said it yourself", 26:25). (4) At the table he adds φάγετε ("eat"), turns the drinking into a command, adds γάρ ("for"), changes ὑπὲρ πολλῶν ("for many", Mark 14:24) to περὶ πολλῶν ("for many"), and adds εἰς ἄφεσιν ἁμαρτιῶν ("for forgiveness of sins") `[T]`. (5) He adds ἀπ' ἄρτι ("from now on"), μεθ' ὑμῶν ("with you") and τοῦ πατρός μου ("of My Father") to the vow (26:29; Mark 14:25 has τοῦ θεοῦ, "of God"). (6) He adds ἐν ἐμοὶ ἐν τῇ νυκτὶ ταύτῃ ("because of Me this night") and τῆς ποίμνης ("of the flock") to the shepherd saying (26:31). Luke 22:20 has ἡ καινὴ διαθήκη ("the new covenant") and no forgiveness clause `[T]`. Each change in (4)–(5) makes the cup more explicit: what it is for, who drinks, and who will drink it again with them `[I]`.

**Tool 11.**

**Exod 24:8 → 26:28** *(high)*. *Source context:* at Sinai, after the people have said twice "All the words which the LORD has spoken we will do" (Exod 24:3, 7), Moses throws the blood on the people. WLC הִנֵּה דַם־הַבְּרִית אֲשֶׁר כָּרַת יְהוָה עִמָּכֶם עַל כָּל־הַדְּבָרִים הָאֵלֶּה ("behold the blood of the covenant which the LORD has cut with you concerning all these words"). Swete and Rahlfs alike: Ἰδοὺ τὸ αἷμα τῆς διαθήκης ἧς διέθετο Κύριος πρὸς ὑμᾶς περὶ πάντων τῶν λόγων τούτων ("Behold the blood of the covenant which the Lord made with you concerning all these words") `[T]`. τὸ αἷμα τῆς διαθήκης ("the blood of the covenant") stands in Swete only at Exod 24:8 `[T]`, verified (and in Rahlfs at the same verse). Then the elders go up, see God, "and they ate and drank" (24:11). *Book usage:* the first Exodus covenant text in Matthew; Exodus is live from 2:15–20 (Exod 4:19) to the mountain of 17:1–8. *OT-to-OT:* Zech 9:11 ("because of the blood of your covenant I have set your prisoners free") reads Exod 24:8 as release; Zech 9:9 has already been cited at 21:5 *(moderate, Move 1)*. *What it adds:* the Sinai rite was blood on the people and a meal before God. Jesus' "this is" replaces Moses' "behold"; the blood is "My blood"; and the people now drink it. **Text-first extension:** Matthew's περί ("for"), which replaces Mark's ὑπέρ, is the preposition of Exod 24:8's περὶ πάντων τῶν λόγων ("concerning all these words") as well as of the Levitical sin offering `[S: audit]`. The coincidence is real; its significance is uncertain. Matthew stands with all three texts here; no divergence.

**Isa 53:11–12 + Dan 9:27 + Lev 4 → 26:28 ("many"; "sins")** `[S: audit]`. Accepted as audited: Isa 53 is **high as a component, moderate as the source**; the cup is a composite. Swete Isa 53:12 αὐτὸς ἁμαρτίας πολλῶν ἀνήνεγκεν ("he bore the sins of many"); WLC חֵטְא־רַבִּים נָשָׂא ("he bore the sin of many"). Dan 9:27 WLC בְּרִית לָרַבִּים ("a covenant for the many"); Theodotion (Swete Dat 9:27) διαθήκην πολλοῖς ("a covenant for many"); OG ἡ διαθήκη εἰς πολλούς ("the covenant for many") `[T]`. **Text-first note:** Dan 9:27 is the only one of the three that has *covenant* and *many* in one clause in Hebrew and in both Greek forms. Isa 53:12 has *sins* and *many* but no covenant. Matthew's sentence needs both `[I]`, moderate–high for the pairing.

**Jer 31:31 → 26:28** *(uncertain–moderate)*. Move 1 only. NA28 and SBLGNT read τῆς διαθήκης ("of the covenant") without καινῆς ("new"); καινῆς is read by A C D K W Γ Δ ƒ¹·¹³ 𝔪 latt sy, and the apparatus marks it "p)", assimilation to the parallels `[T]`. The "new" in Matthew's cup word is the wine, καινόν ("new", 26:29), not the covenant. Jer 31:34 ends "I will forgive their iniquity" `[T]`, so the concept lies near; the wording does not.

**Zech 13:7 → 26:31** *(high)*. *Source context:* WLC חֶרֶב עוּרִי עַל־רֹעִי וְעַל־גֶּבֶר עֲמִיתִי … הַךְ אֶת־הָרֹעֶה וּתְפוּצֶיןָ הַצֹּאן ("Sword, awake against My shepherd, and against the man, My associate … strike the shepherd, and the sheep will be scattered"). The shepherd is the LORD's own, and the man is "My associate" `[T]`. Swete and Rahlfs alike: πατάξατε τοὺς ποιμένας καὶ ἐκσπάσατε τὰ πρόβατα ("strike the shepherds and draw out the sheep") `[T]`. Then a third is refined "as silver is refined", and the LORD says Λαός μου οὗτός ἐστιν ("This is My people", Zech 13:9, Swete) `[T]`. *Book usage:* Zechariah is cited at 21:5 (9:9) and 26:15 (11:12), and will be cited at 27:9–10 (11:12–13); ποιμήν ("shepherd") stands at 9:36; 25:32; 26:31 only (SBLGNT, verified; see the overview). *OT-to-OT:* Zech 11:4–17 (the shepherd valued at thirty pieces) and 13:7 belong to one shepherd cycle; Ezek 34:5–6 is the scattering of shepherdless sheep *(moderate)*. *What it adds:* the scattering is the LORD's own act against his own shepherd, and it leads to a refined remnant called "My people". **Divergence.** Matthew's singular τὸν ποιμένα ("the shepherd") is the Hebrew's (rated high in the overview). Text-first extension: his first person **πατάξω** ("I will strike") is in no text in the corpus; WLC has the imperative הַךְ ("strike"), and Swete and Rahlfs the plural imperative. **The BHS apparatus at Zech 13:7 (note b) records πατάξω ("I will strike") in Greek manuscripts (𝔊ᴹˢˢ), with the Arabic and Armenian, and cites Mt 26,31** `[T]`. So Matthew's form has Greek manuscript support that the editions do not print. Classification: the NT's own Greek, or a minority Greek text; not a translation loss. Mark 14:27 already has πατάξω; Matthew inherits it and adds τῆς ποίμνης ("of the flock") `[T]`.

**Ps 41:10 → Mark 14:18** *(moderate, in Mark)*. Matthew omits the phrase that carries it (see the Synoptic check). Not live in Matthew.

*Internal:* **Answers §1:21** — "his people … their sins" explained at the table (high, verified for ἁμαρτία). **Answers §3:6, 11** — the forgiveness clause withheld from John's baptism (high on the distribution; `[I]` moderate–high on the design). **Answers §23:35** — ἐκχυννόμενον ("poured out", high, verified). **Plants §27:25** — "his blood on us" (high; see unit 53 and Trap 3). **Answers §20:22, 28** — the cup and the "many" (high; overview). **Answers §14:19; §15:36** — the table words of the feedings (moderate–high, verified). **Answers §18:7** — "woe to the man through whom" (moderate, verified). **Plants §26:56** — "all" will flee (high, verified). **Plants §26:64** and **answers §23:39** — ἀπ' ἄρτι ("from now on", moderate–high, verified). **Plants §28:20** — μεθ' ὑμῶν ("with you", high; overview). **Plants §26:49** — "Rabbi" (high, verified). **Plants §28:7** — προάξω ("I will go ahead of you", 26:32 → 28:7; overview). Opening chapters checked: 1:21 (above); the angel's name-giving is the only earlier place where sins and "his people" stand together (SBLGNT) `[T]`.

**Translations.** The NASB95 and the ESV agree on 26:28 ("My blood of the covenant, which is poured out for many for [the] forgiveness of sins"). At 26:31 the NASB95 has "STRIKE DOWN" and the ESV "strike". At 26:32 the NASB95 has "go ahead of you" and the ESV "go before you"; at 28:7 both keep their own choice, so the link to 28:7 survives in each `[T]`. At 26:29 the NASB95 has "from now on"; the ESV has "again", which loses the ἀπ' ἄρτι ("from now on") chain with 26:64, where the ESV has "from now on" `[T]`. **Pulpit divergence:** the ESV hides the three "from now on" partings at 26:29; the congregation will hear "I will not drink again".

**Difficulty.** *Is the meal a Passover?* The text says so four times (πάσχα, "Passover", 26:2, 17, 18, 19; SBLGNT, verified) `[T]`, but no lamb is mentioned. Options: (a) a Passover meal in which Jesus' words take the place of the lamb's interpretation `[I]`; (b) a meal in Passover week, with the Synoptic and Johannine chronologies reconciled in one of several ways `[S]`. Matthew's own interest is the covenant blood, not the lamb.

**Pitfall.** "The Lord's Supper as a memorial of a past event." Matthew has no "do this in remembrance" (contrast Luke 22:19). His additions point the other way: what the blood is *for* (forgiveness), who must drink it (all), and when it will be drunk again (with them, in the Father's kingdom, 26:29). *Corrective:* preach the cup as covenant gift and forgiveness given to deserters, with a forward promise. Preach Matthew's table in Matthew's terms, not Luke's or Paul's.

**Book-overview note:** **confirms with something new.** The overview rates Exod 24:8 high and has the Isa 53 composite `[S: audit]`. Text-first adds: (1) **the forgiveness clause has been moved from John's baptism** (Mark 1:4; Luke 3:3) to the cup; the overview notes that ἄφεσις occurs only at 26:28 but not this Synoptic fact. (2) ἐκχυννόμενον ("poured out") 23:35 → 26:28 is not in the Echo Table. (3) The BHS apparatus note at Zech 13:7 (𝔊ᴹˢˢ πατάξω, citing Mt 26,31) **qualifies** the overview's 26:31 row: Matthew's first person is attested in Greek manuscripts, so this is not purely a "Hebrew against both Greek texts" case; the *singular* is with the Hebrew, the *person* with a minority Greek text. (4) New internal echoes: ἀπ' ἄρτι ("from now on", 23:39; 26:29, 64), 18:7 ↔ 26:24, the feeding words, and συντάσσω ("to direct", 21:6; 26:19; 27:10).

---

## 51. Matthew 26:36–56 — Gethsemane: Your Will Be Done ⭐

**Position.** *Q1:* at the table Jesus foretold that all would stumble (σκανδαλισθήσεσθε, "you will all fall away", 26:31) and Peter would deny him three times (26:34); Peter answered that he would die with him (26:35) `[T]`. *Q2:* this unit exists here because the prediction now begins to come true, and because the Son must pray the prayer he taught before he is handed over. The watch commanded in the last discourse (24:42; 25:13) is now asked for, and not kept `[T]`. The unit ends with the disciples' flight (26:56), the first fulfilment of 26:31 `[T]`.

**Structure.** Into Gethsemane: the three taken aside (26:36–38); three prayers and three returns (26:39–46); the arrest: the kiss, the sword, the Scriptures, the flight (26:47–56) `[T]`.

**Text-first findings.**

- **"Your will be done."** γενηθήτω τὸ θέλημά σου ("Your will be done", 26:42) occurs only at 6:10 and 26:42 (SBLGNT phrase search) `[T]`, verified. Matthew changes Mark 14:36 to give Jesus' second prayer in the words of the prayer he taught `[T]`. The overview's Christological Trajectory (Pattern) rests on this chain; confirmed. Both prayers begin Πάτερ μου ("My Father", 26:39, 42), against Mark's Αββα ὁ πατήρ ("Abba! Father!") `[T]`.
- **"Keep watch with me."** γρηγορεῖτε μετ' ἐμοῦ ("keep watch with Me", 26:38); οὐκ ἰσχύσατε μίαν ὥραν γρηγορῆσαι μετ' ἐμοῦ; ("could you not keep watch with Me for one hour?", 26:40); γρηγορεῖτε καὶ προσεύχεσθε ("keep watching and praying", 26:41) `[T]`. γρηγορέω occurs at 24:42, 43; 25:13; 26:38, 40, 41 (SBLGNT) `[T]`, verified. Matthew alone has μετ' ἐμοῦ ("with Me") here twice (Mark 14:34 has no "with Me") — the Immanuel words (μεθ' ἡμῶν, 1:23) turned into a request `[T]`/`[I]`, moderate.
- **"Lead us not into temptation."** ἵνα μὴ εἰσέλθητε εἰς πειρασμόν ("so that you will not come into temptation", 26:41) — the sixth petition (6:13, μὴ εἰσενέγκῃς ἡμᾶς εἰς πειρασμόν) `[T]`. πειρασμός ("temptation") occurs only at 6:13 and 26:41 (SBLGNT) `[T]`, verified. The flesh ἀσθενής ("weak", 26:41) is the word for the sick visited in 25:43–44 `[T]`, verified.
- **The cup.** παρελθάτω ἀπ' ἐμοῦ τὸ ποτήριον τοῦτο ("let this cup pass from Me", 26:39). The cup offered to the sons of Zebedee (20:22–23) and shared at the table (26:27) is the cup he asks to pass `[T]`, verified (ποτήριον chain). εἰ δυνατόν ἐστιν ("if it is possible") — but παρὰ δὲ θεῷ πάντα δυνατά ("with God all things are possible", 19:26) `[T]`, verified. The Father who can do all things does not remove this cup `[I]`, high.
- **"Friend."** Ἑταῖρε, ἐφ' ὃ πάρει ("Friend, do what you have come for", 26:50). ἑταῖρος ("friend, companion") occurs only at 20:13; 22:12; 26:50 (SBLGNT) `[T]`, verified: the grumbling labourer, the guest without a wedding garment, Judas. Each time it is spoken to someone in the wrong `[I]`, high.
- **The sword and the twelve legions.** πάντες γὰρ οἱ λαβόντες μάχαιραν ἐν μαχαίρῃ ἀπολοῦνται ("all those who take up the sword shall perish by the sword", 26:52) — Matthew's addition `[T]`. μάχαιρα ("sword") stands at 10:34 ("not peace, but a sword") and in this scene only (26:47, 51, 52 three times, 55) `[T]`, verified. δώδεκα λεγιῶνας ἀγγέλων ("twelve legions of angels", 26:53): the devil's offer of angels (4:6) is refused again `[I]`, moderate–high.
- **"That the Scriptures might be fulfilled."** πῶς οὖν πληρωθῶσιν αἱ γραφαὶ ὅτι οὕτως δεῖ γενέσθαι; ("how then will the Scriptures be fulfilled, which say that it must happen this way?", 26:54) and ἵνα πληρωθῶσιν αἱ γραφαὶ τῶν προφητῶν ("to fulfil the Scriptures of the prophets", 26:56) `[T]`. πληρόω ("to fulfil") at 26:54, 56 (overview microscript, 16 verses, verified) — Jesus' own fulfilment sayings, no formula citation `[T]`.
- **They left him and fled.** οἱ μαθηταὶ πάντες ἀφέντες αὐτὸν ἔφυγον ("all the disciples left Him and fled", 26:56). ἀφέντες ("leaving") stands at 4:20, 22; 22:22; 26:56 (SBLGNT phrase search) `[T]`, verified: they left their nets and followed him (4:20, 22); now they leave him `[T]`. The Mark sweep found the same reversal in Mark; Matthew keeps it `[S]`.
- **Robbers.** ὡς ἐπὶ λῃστήν ("as you would against a robber", 26:55). λῃστής ("robber") occurs at 21:13; 26:55; 27:38, 44 (SBLGNT) `[T]`, verified: the temple made a "den of robbers", Jesus arrested as a robber, crucified between robbers `[I]`, moderate–high.

**Synoptic check.** Mark 14:32–52. Matthew adds μετ' ἐμοῦ (26:38, 40), gives the second prayer in direct speech with the Lord's Prayer's words (26:42), adds "Friend, do what you have come for" (26:50), the sword saying and the twelve legions (26:52–54), and "the Scriptures of the prophets" (26:56). He omits Mark's young man who fled naked (Mark 14:51–52) `[T]`.

**Tool 11.** **Ps 42:6, 12; 43:5 (Swete 41:6, 12; 42:5) → 26:38** *(moderate)*. Swete ἵνα τί περίλυπος εἶ, ἡ ψυχή; ("why are you very sorrowful, O soul?") `[T]`. περίλυπος ("very sorrowful") occurs only at 26:38 in Matthew `[T]`. *Source context:* the exile's lament, far from the house of God, ending each time "hope in God". *What it adds:* Jesus prays the lament of the righteous sufferer. Confirms the overview's row.

**Zech 13:7 → 26:31** — see unit 50; the flight at 26:56 fulfils it within the narrative `[T]`.

*Internal:* **answers §6:10, 13** — the prayer prayed (high, verified). **Answers §24:42; §25:13** — the watch (high, verified). **Answers §20:22–23** — the cup (high, verified). **Answers §4:20–22** — the call undone (high, verified). **Answers §4:6** — angels refused (moderate–high). **Answers §10:34** — the sword (moderate). **Plants §27:38, 44** — robbers (moderate–high, verified). **Plants §26:69–75** — Peter's denial (high).

**Translations.** 26:41 πρόθυμον: NASB95 "willing", ESV "willing". 26:50 Ἑταῖρε, ἐφ' ὃ πάρει: NASB95 "Friend, do what you have come for"; ESV "Friend, do what you came to do" `[T]`. **Pulpit divergence:** none bearing.

**Pitfall.** Preaching Gethsemane as a model of how to cope with stress. The cup is the cup of 20:22 and 26:27, and Jesus drinks it for the "many" (26:28). The corrective: preach the prayer as the Son's obedience on our behalf, and the disciples' sleep as what we are without it.

**Book-overview note:** confirms the "your will be done" pattern, the Ps 42–43 row and the watch chain. Extends: ἑταῖρος (20:13; 22:12; 26:50), λῃστής (21:13 → 27:44), ἀφέντες (4:20–22 ↔ 26:56) and μετ' ἐμοῦ (26:38, 40) for the Echo Table.

---

## 52. Matthew 26:57–75 — The Confession and the Denial

**Position.** *Q1:* the disciples have fled (26:56), but Peter follows "at a distance" to the high priest's courtyard (26:58) `[T]`. *Q2:* this unit exists here because two trials run at once: Jesus before the Sanhedrin, where he confesses who he is, and Peter in the courtyard, where he denies he knows him. Matthew tells them in one frame, and the cock's crow ties the second to Jesus' word (26:34, 75) `[T]`/`[I]`, high.

**Structure.** Jesus led to Caiaphas; Peter follows (26:57–58); false witnesses and the temple saying (26:59–62); the oath, the confession, the verdict (26:63–66); the abuse and the mockery "Prophesy!" (26:67–68); Peter's three denials and the cock (26:69–75) `[T]`. Peter's place frames the trial: ἐκάθητο … ἰδεῖν τὸ τέλος ("sat down … to see the outcome", 26:58) and ἐκάθητο ἔξω ἐν τῇ αὐλῇ ("was sitting outside in the courtyard", 26:69) `[T]`.

**Text-first findings.**

- **False witness, as the heart produces it.** ἐζήτουν ψευδομαρτυρίαν ("they kept trying to obtain false testimony", 26:59). ψευδομαρτυρία ("false testimony") occurs only at 15:19 and 26:59 (SBLGNT) `[T]`, verified. What comes "out of the heart" (15:19) is now sought by the court `[I]`, moderate–high. Two come forward at last (δύο, 26:60): the number Deut 19:15 requires (cf. 18:16) `[T]`/`[I]`, moderate.
- **The temple and three days.** Δύναμαι καταλῦσαι τὸν ναὸν τοῦ θεοῦ καὶ διὰ τριῶν ἡμερῶν οἰκοδομῆσαι ("I am able to destroy the temple of God and to rebuild it in three days", 26:61). ναός ("sanctuary") occurs in 8 verses (SBLGNT): 23:16–21, 35; 26:61; 27:5, 40, 51 `[T]`, verified (overview English table). The charge returns at the cross (27:40) and the veil of the ναός is torn (27:51) `[T]`.
- **Silence, then the oath.** ὁ δὲ Ἰησοῦς ἐσιώπα ("but Jesus kept silent", 26:63). The high priest: Ἐξορκίζω σε κατὰ τοῦ θεοῦ τοῦ ζῶντος ("I adjure You by the living God") — the words of Peter's confession, ὁ υἱὸς τοῦ θεοῦ τοῦ ζῶντος (16:16), turned into an oath `[T]`. Jesus answers Σὺ εἶπας ("you have said it", 26:64), as he answered Judas (26:25) `[T]`.
- **"From now on you will see."** ἀπ' ἄρτι ὄψεσθε τὸν υἱὸν τοῦ ἀνθρώπου ("from now on you will see the Son of Man", 26:64). ἀπ' ἄρτι ("from now on") occurs only at 23:39; 26:29; 26:64 (SBLGNT phrase search) `[T]`, verified: "you will not see me from now on until you say 'Blessed is he who comes'" (23:39); "I will not drink from now on" (26:29); "from now on you will see" (26:64). *Synthetic*, high for the chain. Matthew adds ἀπ' ἄρτι to Mark 14:62 `[T]`.
- **Guilty: the antitheses reversed.** ἔνοχος θανάτου ἐστίν ("He deserves death", 26:66). ἔνοχος ("liable, guilty") occurs only at 5:21–22 (four times) and 26:66 (SBLGNT) `[T]`, verified. The one who taught that anger makes a man ἔνοχος τῇ κρίσει ("guilty before the court", 5:22) is declared ἔνοχος by the court `[I]`, moderate–high.
