- **Spitting and slapping: the Sermon enacted.** ἐνέπτυσαν εἰς τὸ πρόσωπον αὐτοῦ καὶ ἐκολάφισαν αὐτόν, οἱ δὲ ἐράπισαν ("they spat in His face and beat Him with their fists; and others slapped Him", 26:67). ῥαπίζω ("to slap") occurs only at 5:39 and 26:67 (SBLGNT) `[T]`, verified (overview Echo Table). The Isaianic colouring is inherited from Mark `[S: audit]`.
- **"Prophesy to us, Christ."** Προφήτευσον ἡμῖν, χριστέ, τίς ἐστιν ὁ παίσας σε; ("Prophesy to us, You Christ; who is the one who hit You?", 26:68). At that moment his prophecy about Peter is being fulfilled in the courtyard `[I]`, high. "Who struck you?" is Matthew's (and Luke's) addition to Mark 14:65 `[T]`.
- **Peter denies, swears, and weeps.** ἠρνήσατο ("he denied", 26:70, 72), μετὰ ὅρκου ("with an oath", 26:72), καταθεματίζειν καὶ ὀμνύειν ("to curse and swear", 26:74) `[T]`. ἀρνέομαι ("to deny") occurs at 10:33 (twice) and 26:70, 72 (SBLGNT) `[T]`, verified: "whoever denies Me before men, I will also deny him" (10:33). ὀμνύω ("to swear") stands at 5:34, 36 ("do not swear at all"), in the woe on oaths (23:16–22), and at 26:74 `[T]`, verified. His words are οὐκ οἶδα τὸν ἄνθρωπον ("I do not know the man", 26:72, 74); see unit 46 for οὐκ οἶδα at 25:12 `[T]`, verified.
- **"The Galilean", "the Nazarene".** Ἰησοῦ τοῦ Γαλιλαίου (26:69) and Ἰησοῦ τοῦ Ναζωραίου (26:71) `[T]`. Ναζωραῖος ("Nazarene") occurs only at 2:23 and 26:71 (SBLGNT) `[T]`, verified: the formula citation's "He shall be called a Nazarene" is fulfilled on a servant-girl's lips `[I]`, moderate.

**Synoptic check.** Mark 14:53–72. Matthew names Caiaphas (26:57), gives the oath "by the living God" (26:63), adds ἀπ' ἄρτι (26:64), "who struck you?" (26:68), the oath at the second denial (26:72), and πικρῶς ("bitterly", 26:75, also Luke 22:62). He has one cockcrow, not Mark's two `[T]`.

**Tool 11.** **Ps 110:1 + Dan 7:13 → 26:64** *(high)*. καθήμενον ἐκ δεξιῶν τῆς δυνάμεως ("sitting at the right hand of power") — Ps 110:1, already cited at 22:44; ἐρχόμενον ἐπὶ τῶν νεφελῶν τοῦ οὐρανοῦ ("coming on the clouds of heaven") — the Old Greek's ἐπί ("on"), not Theodotion's μετά ("with") `[T]` (overview row, confirmed). *Book usage:* Ps 110:1 was the riddle the Pharisees could not answer (22:41–46); here it is the claim that condemns him `[T]`. *OT-to-OT:* Daniel's figure comes to the Ancient of Days and is given dominion; the psalm's lord sits at the LORD's right hand — both enthronement texts `[I]`, high. *What it adds:* the judged will be the judge (cf. 25:31).

**Isa 50:6 → 26:67** `[S: audit]` — high for the passion tradition, moderate for Matthew's own wording; Mic 5:1 Swete for the blow *(a candidate raised by the audit, not yet audited — queue #10)*. **Isa 53:7 → 26:63** *(moderate — Move 1 only)*: the Servant "did not open his mouth" (Swete οὐκ ἀνοίγει τὸ στόμα) `[T]`; Matthew's ἐσιώπα ("kept silent") does not share the words. Not in the overview; candidate, moderate.

*Internal:* **answers §16:16** — "the Son of the living God", now under oath (high). **Answers §5:21–22** — ἔνοχος (moderate–high, verified). **Answers §5:39** — ῥαπίζω (high, verified). **Answers §5:34–37; §10:33** — oaths and denial (high, verified). **Answers §15:19** — false witness (moderate–high, verified). **Answers §2:23** — Nazarene (moderate, verified). **Answers §26:34** — the prediction (high). **Answers §23:39; §26:29** — ἀπ' ἄρτι (high, verified). **Plants §27:40, 51** — the temple (high, verified).

**Translations.** 26:64 ἀπ' ἄρτι: NASB95 "hereafter", ESV "from now on" `[T]`. 26:66 ἔνοχος: NASB95 "He deserves death!", ESV "He deserves death." — both lose the link to "guilty" at 5:21–22 `[T]`. **Pulpit divergence:** the ESV's "from now on" is clearer than the NASB95's "hereafter", which can sound like "in the hereafter".

**Pitfall.** Making the unit Peter's story only. Matthew sets Peter's denial inside Jesus' confession, and the cock crows on Jesus' word. The corrective: preach the two trials together; the one who confesses is condemned so that the one who denies can be restored (28:7, 10, 16).

**Book-overview note:** confirms the Dan 7 / Ps 110 row, the ῥαπίζω echo and the ναός table row. Extends with verified chains: ἀπ' ἄρτι (23:39; 26:29, 64), ἔνοχος (5:21–22 ↔ 26:66), ἀρνέομαι (10:33 ↔ 26:70–72), ὀμνύω (5:34 ↔ 26:74), ψευδομαρτυρία (15:19 ↔ 26:59), Ναζωραῖος (2:23 ↔ 26:71).

---

## 53. Matthew 27:1–26 — Judas, Pilate, and the Blood ⭐

**Position.** *Q1:* the Sanhedrin has found Jesus "deserving of death" (26:66), and Peter has denied him (26:75) `[T]`. *Q2:* this unit exists here because the verdict must be carried out by the Gentile governor (20:19, "they will hand Him over to the Gentiles"), and because Matthew sets Judas' end inside the trial. The unit is a meditation on blood: innocent blood betrayed (27:4), blood-money (27:6), the Field of Blood (27:8), Pilate's "innocent of this blood" (27:24) and the people's "his blood on us" (27:25) `[T]`/`[I]`, high. It answers the woe of 23:35, "upon you may fall the guilt of all the righteous blood" `[T]`.

**Structure.** The morning council and the handing over (27:1–2); Judas: remorse, the silver, the hanging, the field, the formula (27:3–10); Pilate's question and Jesus' silence (27:11–14); Barabbas, the dream, the crowd (27:15–23); the hand-washing, the cry, the sentence (27:24–26) `[T]`. The Judas scene interrupts the Roman trial, so that the blood theme runs through both `[I]`, high.

**Text-first findings.**

- **Judas "changed his mind".** μεταμεληθείς ("he felt remorse", 27:3). μεταμέλομαι ("to change one's mind, regret") occurs only at 21:29, 32; 27:3 (SBLGNT) `[T]`, verified: the son who "regretted it and went" (21:29), the leaders who did not "change your minds afterward so as to believe" John (21:32), and Judas, whose regret ends in death `[I]`, moderate–high.
- **Innocent blood.** Ἥμαρτον παραδοὺς αἷμα ἀθῷον ("I have sinned by betraying innocent blood", 27:4). ἀθῷος ("innocent") occurs only at 27:4 and 27:24 (SBLGNT) `[T]`, verified. Judas and Pilate, the two who hand him over, both declare his innocence `[T]`. The chief priests answer Τί πρὸς ἡμᾶς; σὺ ὄψῃ ("What is that to us? See to that yourself!", 27:4); Pilate answers the crowd ὑμεῖς ὄψεσθε ("see to that yourselves", 27:24) `[T]`. The responsibility is passed from hand to hand `[I]`, high (overview English table, the ὄψεσθε chain).
- **The silver.** ἀργύριον ("silver, money") stands at 25:18, 27; 26:15; 27:3, 5, 6, 9; 28:12, 15 (SBLGNT) `[T]`, verified. Thirty pieces bought the betrayal (26:15) and the field (27:7); ἀργύρια ἱκανά ("a large sum of money", 28:12) buys the guards' lie `[T]`. **The money that bought the Son's death buys the silence about his resurrection.** *Synthetic*, high for the chain.
- **The council again.** συμβούλιον ἔλαβον ("they conferred together", 27:1, 7) — the third and fourth uses of the plot word that began at 12:14 `[T]`, verified.
- **A burial place for strangers.** εἰς ταφὴν τοῖς ξένοις ("as a burial place for strangers", 27:7). ξένος ("stranger") occurs at 25:35, 38, 43, 44 and here (SBLGNT) `[T]`, verified. The price of the King's blood buys a grave for the strangers he called "the least of these my brothers" (25:35, 40) `[I]`, moderate. Named ἕως τῆς σήμερον ("to this day", 27:8) — the narrator writing after the event (overview, Presenting Situation) `[T]`.
- **Pilate's wife and her dream.** τῷ δικαίῳ ἐκείνῳ ("that righteous Man") … κατ' ὄναρ ("in a dream", 27:19). ὄναρ ("dream") occurs only at 1:20; 2:12, 13, 19, 22; 27:19 (SBLGNT) `[T]`, verified: five dreams protect the child (1–2), and the sixth, given to a Gentile woman, declares the man innocent `[T]`. Joseph, the first dreamer, was δίκαιος ("righteous", 1:19) `[T]`.
- **"Jesus who is called Christ".** Τί οὖν ποιήσω Ἰησοῦν τὸν λεγόμενον χριστόν; (27:22). NA28 brackets [Ἰησοῦν] before Βαραββᾶν at 27:16–17, read by Θ ƒ¹ 700* syˢ `[S]` (overview §2). If it stands, Pilate offers "Jesus Barabbas or Jesus who is called Christ" `[T]` for the NA28 bracket; the reading is `[S]`-contested. Nothing here rests on it.
- **"All the people".** ἀποκριθεὶς πᾶς ὁ λαὸς εἶπεν· Τὸ αἷμα αὐτοῦ ἐφ' ἡμᾶς καὶ ἐπὶ τὰ τέκνα ἡμῶν ("and all the people said, 'His blood shall be on us and on our children!'", 27:25) `[T]`. λαός ("people") stands in 14 verses (SBLGNT), from αὐτὸς γὰρ σώσει τὸν λαὸν αὐτοῦ ἀπὸ τῶν ἁμαρτιῶν αὐτῶν ("He will save His people from their sins", 1:21) to here `[T]`, verified. The overview's Trap 3 reads 27:25 with 1:21 and 26:28; this run agrees from the text `[I]`, high.
- **The riot that was feared.** θόρυβος ("riot, uproar") occurs only at 26:5 ("lest a riot occur among the people") and 27:24 ("a riot was starting") `[T]`, verified. The leaders feared a riot if they took him; Pilate gives way to one `[I]`, moderate.

**Synoptic check.** Mark 15:1–15. Matthew alone has Judas' remorse and death, the field and the formula citation (27:3–10), Pilate's wife's dream (27:19), the hand-washing and "I am innocent" (27:24), and the people's cry (27:25). He adds "who is called Christ" (27:17, 22) `[T]`.

**Tool 11.** All the major claims here were tested by the claim audit; they are taken as settled and not re-derived.

- **Zech 11:12–13 (+ Jer 18–19; 32) → 27:9–10** *(high for Zechariah; the Jeremiah attribution is an open crux)* `[S: audit]` and overview row. The potter is in the Hebrew (אֶל־הַיּוֹצֵר, "to the potter"), not in either Greek text (εἰς τὸ χωνευτήριον, "into the furnace") `[T]`.
- **2 Sam 17:23 → 27:5** *(high)* `[S: audit]`. Ahithophel, David's counsellor who went over to his enemy, went to his house and hanged himself; ἀπήγξατο occurs in narrative only there and here.
- **Deut 21:6–9 (ritual) → 27:24** `[S: audit]`; **Deut 27:25, 2 Sam 3:28 and Sus Th 46 → 27:24–25** *(moderate; new candidates from the audit, not yet audited — queue #1–2)*. Pilate performs Israel's rite of release over innocent blood (Deut 21:6) and speaks David's and Daniel's formula of innocence (2 Sam 3:28; Sus Th 46); the people's answer stands nearest Deut 27:25, "cursed is he who takes a bribe to strike down an innocent person — and all the people shall say Amen" `[T]`; the link is a candidate, moderate. The text-first work here adds one link: Judas' bribe (26:15; 27:3) is the bribe of Deut 27:25, so the curse the people pronounce there belongs first to the bribed and the bribers `[I]`, moderate.

*Internal:* **answers §23:35** — "all the righteous blood … upon you" (high, verified for αἷμα; the reading is `[I]`). **Answers §1:21; §26:28** — "his people", "blood poured out for the forgiveness of sins" (high). **Answers §1:19–2:22** — the sixth dream (high, verified). **Answers §21:29, 32** — μεταμέλομαι (moderate–high, verified). **Answers §25:35–44** — strangers (moderate, verified). **Answers §26:5** — θόρυβος (moderate, verified). **Plants §28:12–15** — the silver (high, verified).

**Translations.** 27:4, 24 σὺ ὄψῃ / ὑμεῖς ὄψεσθε: NASB95 "See to that yourself!" / "see to that yourselves"; ESV "See to it yourself" / "see to it yourselves" (overview English table; confirmed) `[T]`. 27:25 both "His blood be on us and on our children" in substance `[T]`. **Pulpit divergence:** none bearing.

**Difficulty.** 27:25 — **a pastoral landmine** (overview, Trap 3). *Function in context:* the cry of a crowd persuaded by the chief priests and elders (27:20), within a narrative that began with Jesus saving "his people from their sins" (1:21) and has just shown his blood "poured out for many for the forgiveness of sins" (26:28) `[T]`. *Options:* (a) a self-curse that the narrative fulfils in AD 70; (b) dramatic irony — the blood they call down is the blood that forgives; (c) both `[S]`. *What the text supports:* the irony is set up by the book's own words (λαός, αἷμα, ἄφεσις) `[T]`/`[I]`; the speaker is a crowd on one morning, not a people for all time `[I]`, high. *Handling:* name the historic misuse of this verse from the pulpit, and preach it with 1:21 and 26:28.

**Pitfall.** Preaching Pilate as the villain and the crowd as "them". Every party in the scene hands the innocent one over — disciple, priests, governor, people — and the narrative includes the reader with them `[I]`. The corrective: preach the blood that is "on us" as the blood shed "for many" (26:28).

**Book-overview note:** confirms the audited rows (Zech 11, 2 Sam 17:23, Deut 21/27), Trap 3 and the ὄψεσθε and ἀθῷος chains. Extends with verified chains: ἀργύριον (26:15 → 28:12–15), μεταμέλομαι (21:29, 32 ↔ 27:3), ὄναρ (1:20 → 27:19), θόρυβος (26:5 ↔ 27:24).

---

## 54. Matthew 27:27–56 — The King of the Jews Crucified; "Truly God's Son" ⭐

**Position.** *Q1:* Pilate has handed Jesus over to be crucified (27:26) `[T]`. *Q2:* this unit exists here because the King must be enthroned as the book has defined kingship: crowned with thorns, titled on a cross, flanked by robbers at his right and left (cf. 20:21–23), and confessed as God's Son by Gentile soldiers. The devil's condition from the wilderness, "if you are the Son of God", returns at the cross (4:3, 6 → 27:40) `[T]`. The unit is the climax of the book's Son-of-God and King-of-the-Jews threads `[I]`, high.

**Structure.** The soldiers' mock coronation (27:27–31); Simon, Golgotha, the wine with gall, the garments, the title, the robbers (27:32–38); three groups of mockers (27:39–44); darkness, the cry, Elijah, the sour wine, the death (27:45–50); the veil, the earthquake, the tombs, the centurion's confession (27:51–54); the women watching (27:55–56) `[T]`.

**Text-first findings.**

- **The King is crowned and titled.** στέφανον ἐξ ἀκανθῶν ("a crown of thorns"), κάλαμον ("a reed") in his right hand, Χαῖρε, βασιλεῦ τῶν Ἰουδαίων ("Hail, King of the Jews!", 27:29); over his head Οὗτός ἐστιν Ἰησοῦς ὁ βασιλεὺς τῶν Ἰουδαίων ("This is Jesus the King of the Jews", 27:37) `[T]`. "King of the Jews" occurs only at 2:2; 27:11, 29, 37 (SBLGNT; 27:29 vocative), each time in Gentile mouths (overview, verified) `[T]`. The chief priests say βασιλεὺς Ἰσραήλ ("King of Israel", 27:42) `[T]`.
- **The reed.** κάλαμος ("reed") stands at 11:7; 12:20; 27:29, 30, 48 (SBLGNT) `[T]`, verified. The Servant who would not break κάλαμον συντετριμμένον ("a battered reed", 12:20) is given a reed for a sceptre, struck on the head with it (27:30), and offered sour wine on one (27:48) `[I]`, moderate–high. *Synthetic*.
- **His head.** κεφαλή ("head") stands at 8:20 ("the Son of Man has nowhere to lay His head"); 21:42 (the stone that became κεφαλὴν γωνίας, "the chief corner stone"); 27:29, 30, 37 (the thorns, the blows, the title over his head) and 27:39 (the passers-by shaking their heads) `[T]`, verified. *Synthetic*, moderate.
- **The devil's "if" returns.** εἰ υἱὸς εἶ τοῦ θεοῦ, κατάβηθι ἀπὸ τοῦ σταυροῦ ("if You are the Son of God, come down from the cross", 27:40) — the condition of 4:3, 6 (overview Christological Trajectory; confirmed) `[T]`. Matthew alone adds it to Mark 15:30 `[T]`. And 27:43: εἶπεν γὰρ ὅτι Θεοῦ εἰμι υἱός ("for He said, 'I am the Son of God'"), again Matthew's addition `[T]`.
- **"The holy city".** ἡ ἁγία πόλις ("the holy city") occurs only at 4:5 and 27:53 (SBLGNT phrase search) `[T]`, verified. The devil set him on the temple's pinnacle in the holy city and offered him a spectacle; after his resurrection the saints enter the holy city alive (27:53) `[I]`, moderate–high. *Synthetic*.
- **The cry.** Ἠλὶ ἠλὶ λεμὰ σαβαχθάνι; … Θεέ μου θεέ μου, ἱνατί με ἐγκατέλιπες; ("Eli, Eli, lama sabachthani? … My God, My God, why have You forsaken Me?", 27:46) `[T]`. ηλι is the Hebrew אֵלִי of Ps 22:2; Mark has the Aramaic ἐλωΐ (overview; confirmed). The bystanders hear Ἠλίαν ("Elijah", 27:47) — the name Jesus gave John (11:14; 17:12) `[T]`.
- **Rock, tomb, earthquake.** ἡ γῆ ἐσείσθη, καὶ αἱ πέτραι ἐσχίσθησαν, καὶ τὰ μνημεῖα ἀνεῴχθησαν ("the earth shook and the rocks were split. The tombs were opened", 27:51–52) `[T]`. Matthew alone has this. σεισμός ("earthquake", 27:54) and the σεισμός at the tomb (28:2) — see unit 16 for the chain (8:24; 24:7; 27:54; 28:2) `[T]`, verified. πέτρα ("rock") stands at 7:24, 25; 16:18; 27:51, 60 `[T]`, verified.
- **"Truly this was God's Son."** Ἀληθῶς θεοῦ υἱὸς ἦν οὗτος (27:54) — the echo of 14:33 (overview, verified) `[T]`. Matthew's centurion is joined by οἱ μετ' αὐτοῦ ("those who were with him"), and they ἐφοβήθησαν σφόδρα ("became very frightened") at the earthquake `[T]` — Matthew's change of Mark 15:39, where the centurion sees how he died `[T]`.
- **The women: following and serving.** ἠκολούθησαν τῷ Ἰησοῦ ἀπὸ τῆς Γαλιλαίας διακονοῦσαι αὐτῷ ("who had followed Jesus from Galilee while ministering to Him", 27:55) `[T]`. ἀκολουθέω ("to follow") and διακονέω ("to serve") — the two disciple verbs — are given to the women at the cross while the Twelve have fled (26:56) `[T]`. The mother of the sons of Zebedee is there (27:56; cf. 20:20) `[T]`.

**Synoptic check.** Mark 15:16–41. Matthew adds: χολή ("gall") for Mark's myrrh (27:34) `[S: audit]`; "if You are the Son of God" (27:40) and "for He said, 'I am the Son of God'" (27:43); Ἠλί for ἐλωΐ (27:46); the earthquake, the split rocks, the opened tombs and the raised saints (27:51–53); the centurion's companions and their fear (27:54). He drops Mark's "Alexander and Rufus" (Mark 15:21) `[T]`.

**Tool 11.** **Ps 22 (Swete 21) → 27:35, 39, 43, 46** *(high)*. The passion is told in the psalm: the divided garments (22:19), the wagging heads (22:8), "he trusted in God; let him deliver him" (22:9), and the cry (22:2) `[T]` (overview row, confirmed). *Source context:* the psalm ends in praise and in "all the families of the nations" worshipping (22:28) `[T]`; the Gentile confession of 27:54 follows the cry `[I]`, moderate–high.

**Ps 69:22 → 27:34, 48** *(high)* `[S: audit]`. Gall and sour wine, both lines of the verse.

**Wis 2:18 → 27:43** *(uncertain–moderate; outside the Tanak)* — overview row; not re-tested.

**Ezek 37:12–13 → 27:52–53** *(moderate)*. Swete ἀνοίγω ὑμῶν τὰ μνήματα ("I will open your graves") … εἰσάξω ὑμᾶς εἰς τὴν γῆν τοῦ Ἰσραήλ ("I will bring you into the land of Israel") `[T]` (overview row). Matthew: τὰ μνημεῖα ἀνεῴχθησαν … εἰσῆλθον εἰς τὴν ἁγίαν πόλιν ("the tombs were opened … they entered the holy city") `[T]`. Moderate; confirmed.

**Amos 8:9 → 27:45** *(moderate)*. Darkness at noon; the Mark audit found Exod 10:22 Swete verbally closer (σκότος … ἐπὶ πᾶσαν γῆν, "darkness … over all the land") `[S]` (Mark claim audit). Matthew's wording is Mark's `[T]`. Not in the Matthew overview; candidate, moderate.

*Internal:* **answers §4:3–6** — "if you are the Son of God"; the holy city (high, verified). **Answers §2:2** — King of the Jews (high, verified). **Answers §12:20** — the reed (moderate–high, verified). **Answers §14:33** — the confession (high, verified). **Answers §20:21–23** — right and left (high, verified). **Answers §11:14; §17:12** — Elijah (high). **Answers §26:61** — the temple (high). **Answers §26:56** — the women follow where the men fled (high). **Plants §28:2** — earthquake (high, verified).

**Translations.** 27:54 NASB95 "Truly this was the Son of God!"; ESV "Truly this was the Son of God!" — both keep the article English needs; the Greek has none (overview English table) `[T]`. 27:46 NASB95 "ELI, ELI"; ESV "Eli, Eli" `[T]`. **Text:** NA28 does not print the spear-thrust sentence at 27:49 (read by א B C L Γ, borrowed from John 19:34) `[S]` (overview §5). **Pulpit divergence:** none bearing.

**Difficulty.** 27:52–53, the raised saints. *Function in context:* a sign that the death has opened the graves, placed before the centurion's confession; their appearance is dated μετὰ τὴν ἔγερσιν αὐτοῦ ("after His resurrection") `[T]`. Options: literal event; apocalyptic sign; a theological statement of the resurrection's reach `[S]`. Preach what the text claims — that his death opened the tombs — without speculating beyond it.

**Pitfall.** Preaching the cry as the Father turning away in disgust, or explaining it away. The words are the psalm's first line, prayed to "my God" `[T]`; the psalm ends in vindication and the nations' worship, and the narrative answers at once with the torn veil, the opened tombs and a Gentile's confession. The corrective: preach the cry with the psalm entire and with 27:51–54.

**Book-overview note:** confirms the Ps 22, Ps 69, Ezek 37, ηλι and ἀληθῶς θεοῦ υἱός rows. Extends with verified chains: κάλαμος (12:20 → 27:29–48), ἁγία πόλις (4:5 ↔ 27:53), κεφαλή (8:20 → 27:29–39), right and left (20:21–23 ↔ 27:38). Amos 8:9 / Exod 10:22 is a candidate for 27:45.

---

## 55. Matthew 27:57–28:15 — The Tomb, the Guard, the Earthquake, the Lie ⭐

**Position.** *Q1:* Jesus has died, and the women watch from a distance (27:50, 55–56) `[T]`. *Q2:* this unit exists here because the burial, the guard and the empty tomb are told as one story with two sides: the women who come to see and are sent with news, and the chief priests who seal, guard and then bribe. The plot that began at 12:14 ends here, in its fifth συμβούλιον (28:12) `[T]`. The unit sets up the final mountain (28:16–20) `[I]`, high.

**Structure.** The burial by Joseph of Arimathea (27:57–61); the guard and the seal (27:62–66); the earthquake, the angel, the message (28:1–8); Jesus meets the women (28:9–10); the guards' report and the bribe (28:11–15) `[T]`. The unit alternates the two sides: burial — guard — tomb — guard `[T]`.

**Text-first findings.**

- **A rich disciple, a new tomb in the rock.** ἄνθρωπος πλούσιος ("a rich man", 27:57), who ἐμαθητεύθη τῷ Ἰησοῦ ("had also become a disciple of Jesus") `[T]`. μαθητεύω ("to make a disciple") occurs only at 13:52; 27:57; 28:19 (SBLGNT) `[T]`, verified (overview microscript). The last disciple made before the commission is a rich man — after "it is hard for a rich man to enter the kingdom" (19:23) `[I]`, moderate–high. The tomb is ἐν τῇ πέτρᾳ ("in the rock", 27:60): πέτρα at 7:24–25; 16:18; 27:51, 60 `[T]`, verified.
- **"That deceiver said, 'After three days I will rise.'"** ἐκεῖνος ὁ πλάνος εἶπεν ἔτι ζῶν· Μετὰ τρεῖς ἡμέρας ἐγείρομαι (27:63) `[T]`. The enemies remember his prediction better than his disciples do `[I]`, high. ἡ ἐσχάτη πλάνη χείρων τῆς πρώτης ("the last deception will be worse than the first", 27:64) — the words of 12:45 (χείρονα τῶν πρώτων) `[T]`, verified (see unit 24).
- **Seal and guard.** ἀσφαλίζω ("to make secure") at 27:64, 65, 66 and κουστωδία ("guard") at 27:65, 66; 28:11 (SBLGNT) `[T]`, verified. The more the tomb is secured, the more witnesses the resurrection has `[I]`, moderate–high.
- **The earthquake and the angel.** σεισμὸς ἐγένετο μέγας ("a severe earthquake had occurred", 28:2). σεισμός: 8:24; 24:7; 27:54; 28:2 (SBLGNT) `[T]`, verified. The angel's appearance is ὡς ἀστραπή ("like lightning", 28:3) — ἀστραπή occurs only at 24:27 (the coming of the Son of Man "as the lightning") and 28:3 `[T]`, verified. The guards ἐγενήθησαν ὡς νεκροί ("became like dead men", 28:4): the living guards are as the dead, the dead one is risen `[I]`, high.
- **"He has risen, just as He said."** ἠγέρθη γὰρ καθὼς εἶπεν ("for He has risen, just as He said", 28:6). The three predictions (16:21; 17:23; 20:19) and 26:32 are fulfilled "as he said" (overview Echo Table; confirmed) `[T]`. ἐκεῖ αὐτὸν ὄψεσθε ("there you will see Him", 28:7) keeps the promise of 26:32, "after I have been raised, I will go ahead of you to Galilee" `[T]`.
- **Fear and great joy; worship.** μετὰ φόβου καὶ χαρᾶς μεγάλης ("with fear and great joy", 28:8). χαρὰν μεγάλην σφόδρα ("exceedingly great joy", 2:10) was the magi's at the star `[T]`; χαρά occurs at 2:10; 13:20, 44; 25:21, 23; 28:8 `[T]`, verified. The women ἐκράτησαν αὐτοῦ τοὺς πόδας καὶ προσεκύνησαν αὐτῷ ("took hold of His feet and worshipped Him", 28:9) — the first worship of the risen Jesus `[T]`. προσκυνέω (13 verses, SBLGNT) begins with the magi (2:2, 11) and ends at 28:9, 17 `[T]`, verified.
- **"My brothers".** ἀπαγγείλατε τοῖς ἀδελφοῖς μου ("take word to My brethren", 28:10) — the disciples who fled are called his brothers `[T]`. "My brothers" are those who do the Father's will (12:49–50) and "the least of these my brothers" (25:40) `[T]`, verified.
- **The bribe and the story.** συμβούλιόν τε λαβόντες ἀργύρια ἱκανὰ ἔδωκαν τοῖς στρατιώταις ("after consulting together, they gave a large sum of money to the soldiers", 28:12). συμβούλιον: 12:14; 22:15; 27:1, 7; 28:12 (SBLGNT) `[T]`, verified — the fifth and last plot. ἀργύριον: 26:15; 27:3–9; 28:12, 15 `[T]`, verified. διεφημίσθη ὁ λόγος οὗτος ("this story was widely spread", 28:15) — διαφημίζω occurs only at 9:31 (the healed blind men spread his fame) and 28:15 (the guards spread the lie) `[T]`, verified. μέχρι τῆς σήμερον (ἡμέρας) ("to this day") at 11:23 and 28:15 `[T]`, verified.

**Synoptic check.** Mark 15:42–16:8. Matthew makes Joseph "rich" and "a disciple" (27:57), and adds the guard (27:62–66; 28:4, 11–15), the earthquake and the angel rolling the stone and sitting on it (28:2–3), the women's joy (28:8; Mark 16:8 has fear and silence), and the meeting with Jesus (28:9–10) `[T]`. None of the guard material is in Mark or Luke.

**Tool 11.** **Isa 53:9 → 27:57** *(uncertain–moderate)* — overview row; Matthew alone calls Joseph πλούσιος ("rich"); Swete τοὺς πλουσίους ἀντὶ τοῦ θανάτου αὐτοῦ ("the rich for his death") `[T]`. Held as the overview had it.

**Dan 7:9 (Theodotion) → 28:3** *(moderate–high)*. *Source context:* the Ancient of Days takes his throne for judgement, before the one like a son of man is given dominion (7:13–14). Swete's Theodotion: τὸ ἔνδυμα αὐτοῦ ὡσεὶ χιὼν λευκόν ("his clothing white as snow"); the Old Greek has περιβολὴν ὡσεὶ χιόνα ("a garment like snow") `[T]`. Matthew: τὸ ἔνδυμα αὐτοῦ λευκὸν ὡς χιών ("his clothing as white as snow", 28:3) — Theodotion's four words in a different order `[T]`. χιών ("snow") occurs only here in Matthew `[T]`. *Book usage:* Daniel 7 is the source of record for 28:18 (OG 7:14) and for the clouds at 24:30; 26:64 `[S: audit]`. *What it adds:* the messenger of the resurrection wears the Ancient of Days' clothing, three verses before the risen Son of Man claims the Danielic authority. Not in the overview; **new candidate, moderate–high, unaudited**.

*Internal:* **answers §12:40; §16:21; §17:23; §20:19; §26:32** — the resurrection "as he said" (high, verified). **Answers §12:45** — χείρων (high, verified). **Answers §12:14** — the last συμβούλιον (high, verified). **Answers §2:10–11** — joy and worship (high, verified). **Answers §26:15; §27:3–10** — the silver (high, verified). **Answers §24:27** — lightning (moderate, verified). **Answers §9:31** — διαφημίζω (moderate, verified). **Plants §28:16–20** — Galilee, the brothers, worship (high).

**Translations.** 28:10 τοῖς ἀδελφοῖς μου: NASB95 "My brethren", ESV "my brothers" `[T]`. 28:15 NASB95 "this story was widely spread among the Jews, and is to this day"; ESV "this story has been spread among the Jews to this day" `[T]`. **Pulpit divergence:** none bearing. Matthew's Ἰουδαῖοι ("Jews") here is the narrator's one use in his own voice (overview, Presenting Situation) `[T]`; preach it with that care.

**Pitfall.** Treating the guard story as an apologetic appendix. Matthew makes it the last act of the plot that began at 12:14, bought with the same silver as the betrayal. The corrective: preach the empty tomb and the bribed lie together — the resurrection was opposed by money from the start.

**Book-overview note:** confirms the "as he said", συμβούλιον, χείρων and προσκυνέω rows, and the μαθητεύω chain. Extends with verified chains: διαφημίζω (9:31 ↔ 28:15), ἀστραπή (24:27 ↔ 28:3), ἀσφαλίζω / κουστωδία (27:64–28:11). Dan 7:9 Theodotion is a new candidate for 28:3 (moderate–high).

---

## 56. Matthew 28:16–20 — All Authority; All the Nations; Always ⭐

**Position.** *Q1:* the angel and Jesus have sent the women to tell "My brothers" to go to Galilee, "and there they will see Me" (28:7, 10) `[T]`. *Q2:* this unit exists here because it is the end towards which the book has been moving: the mountain in Galilee of the nations, the authority refused from the devil and now given by the Father, the nations once excluded from the mission (10:5–6) now its object, and Immanuel's promise (1:23) spoken by Jesus himself `[T]`/`[I]`, high. There is no ascension; the book ends with him present `[T]` (overview, Canonical Position).

**Structure.** The eleven on the mountain: worship and doubt (28:16–17); Jesus comes near and speaks: authority (28:18), commission (28:19–20a), presence (28:20b) `[T]`. οὖν ("therefore", 28:19) ties the commission to the authority `[T]`.

**Text-first findings.**

- **The mountain.** εἰς τὸ ὄρος οὗ ἐτάξατο αὐτοῖς ὁ Ἰησοῦς ("to the mountain which Jesus had designated", 28:16). ὄρος ("mountain") stands in 16 verses (SBLGNT), from the mountain of temptation (4:8) and the Sermon (5:1) through the Transfiguration (17:1, 9) to here `[T]`, verified. τάσσω ("to designate") occurs only here; no such appointment is narrated earlier `[T]`. The overview's mountain frame (4:8 ↔ 28:16–18) is confirmed `[T]`.
- **Worship and doubt.** προσεκύνησαν, οἱ δὲ ἐδίστασαν ("they worshipped Him; but some were doubtful", 28:17). διστάζω ("to doubt") occurs only at 14:31 and 28:17 (SBLGNT) `[T]`, verified. προσκυνέω (13 verses) ends here `[T]`, verified. The doubting are not excluded from the commission `[T]`.
- **He comes near.** προσελθὼν ὁ Ἰησοῦς ("Jesus came up", 28:18). Jesus is the subject of προσέρχομαι ("to come to") only at 17:7 and 28:18 (overview, verified) `[T]`. At 17:7 he came to the terrified disciples and touched them `[T]`.
- **"All authority has been given to Me."** Ἐδόθη μοι πᾶσα ἐξουσία ἐν οὐρανῷ καὶ ἐπὶ τῆς γῆς (28:18). ἐξουσία ("authority") runs from 7:29 to here (SBLGNT, 9 verses) `[T]`, verified; the Son of Man's authority "on earth" (9:6) is now "in heaven and on earth" `[T]`. The devil offered "all the kingdoms of the world" for worship (4:8–9); the Father gives more, and the disciples worship (overview Echo Table; confirmed) `[T]`/`[I]`.
- **The commission: four "alls".** πᾶσα ἐξουσία ("all authority"), πάντα τὰ ἔθνη ("all the nations"), πάντα ὅσα ἐνετειλάμην ("all that I commanded"), πάσας τὰς ἡμέρας ("all the days", "always") `[T]`. μαθητεύσατε ("make disciples") is the only imperative; πορευθέντες ("going"), βαπτίζοντες ("baptising") and διδάσκοντες ("teaching") are participles `[T]` (overview Trap 6). μαθητεύω occurs only at 13:52; 27:57; 28:19 (SBLGNT) and in the NT once more (Acts 14:21) `[T]`, verified.
- **The name.** εἰς τὸ ὄνομα τοῦ πατρὸς καὶ τοῦ υἱοῦ καὶ τοῦ ἁγίου πνεύματος ("in the name of the Father and the Son and the Holy Spirit", 28:19) `[T]`. The three were present together at the baptism (3:16–17) `[T]`. βαπτίζω ("to baptise") occurs at 3:6, 11, 13, 14, 16 and 28:19 only (SBLGNT) `[T]`, verified: **between the Jordan and the mountain no one in Matthew is baptised.** The book's baptisms begin with John and resume with the church `[I]`, high. ὄνομα ("name") opens the book: καλέσεις τὸ ὄνομα αὐτοῦ Ἰησοῦν ("you shall call His name Jesus", 1:21, 25) and καλέσουσιν τὸ ὄνομα αὐτοῦ Ἐμμανουήλ ("they shall call His name Immanuel", 1:23) `[T]`, verified.
- **"Keep all that I commanded you."** διδάσκοντες αὐτοὺς τηρεῖν πάντα ὅσα ἐνετειλάμην ὑμῖν (28:20). τηρέω ("to keep, watch over") occurs at 19:17 ("keep the commandments"); 23:3; 27:36, 54; 28:4 (the soldiers and guards "keeping watch over" Jesus); 28:20 `[T]`, verified. *Synthetic*, moderate: the guards kept watch over him; the disciples are to keep his words. ἐντέλλομαι ("to command") stands at 4:6 (the devil quoting Ps 91: "He will command His angels"), 17:9; 19:7 (what Moses "commanded"); 28:20 `[T]`, verified — the last use is Jesus' own commands, set where Moses' commands stood (19:7) `[I]`, moderate.
- **"I am with you always."** ἐγὼ μεθ' ὑμῶν εἰμι πάσας τὰς ἡμέρας ἕως τῆς συντελείας τοῦ αἰῶνος ("I am with you always, even to the end of the age", 28:20). The inclusio with 1:23 μεθ' ἡμῶν ὁ θεός ("God with us") is the book's frame (overview; confirmed) `[T]`. συντέλεια τοῦ αἰῶνος ("the end of the age") stands at 13:39, 40, 49; 24:3; 28:20 (συντέλεια, SBLGNT) `[T]`, verified: the harvest (13:39–49), the disciples' question (24:3), and the promise that reaches it (28:20).

**Synoptic check.** The scene is Matthew's alone. Mark 16:7 promises a meeting in Galilee but does not narrate it; Luke's commission is in Jerusalem (Luke 24:44–49) `[T]`.

**Tool 11.** **Dan 7:13–14 (OG) → 28:18–19** *(high)* `[S: audit]` — the source of record (v0.2 overview, Canonical Position). Swete Dan 7:14: ἐδόθη αὐτῷ ἐξουσία … καὶ πάντα τὰ ἔθνη τῆς γῆς ("authority was given to him … and all the nations of the earth") `[T]`. The audit established it; this run agrees, and adds that 28:18 follows the angel in Daniel-7 clothing (28:3, see unit 55) `[I]`, moderate.

**Hag 1:13; 2:4; Gen 28:15 → 28:20** *(moderate–high; raised by the audit as new candidates, not yet audited — queue #16–17)*. Swete Hag 1:13 Ἐγώ εἰμι μεθ' ὑμῶν ("I am with you"), to the rebuilders; Gen 28:15 ἰδοὺ ἐγὼ μετὰ σοῦ … οὐ μή σε ἐγκαταλείπω ἕως … πάντα ὅσα ἐλάλησά σοι ("behold, I am with you … I will not leave you until … all that I have spoken to you"), to Jacob going into exile `[T]`. Matthew's ἕως ("until") and πάντα ὅσα ("all that") are Gen 28:15's `[T]`. The wording supports the candidates; a claim audit should rate them.

**Isa 7:14 / 8:8, 10 → 28:20** *(high, by inclusio)*. The book's first formula citation (1:23) is answered without a formula `[T]`.

**2 Chr 36:23 → 28:18–20** *(low–moderate; order-dependent)* `[S: audit]` — see the overview's Canonical Position (a).

*Internal:* **answers §1:21–23** — the name and the presence (high, verified). **Answers §4:8–10** — the mountain, the kingdoms, worship (high). **Answers §10:5–6; §15:24** — the restriction lifted (high, verified). **Answers §9:6** — authority on earth, now in heaven and on earth (high, verified). **Answers §14:31** — doubt (high, verified). **Answers §17:7** — he comes near (high, verified). **Answers §3:16–17** — the Father, the Son, the Spirit (high). **Answers §24:3, 14** — the end of the age; all the nations (high, verified). **Answers §5:1–7:29** — "all that I commanded" (moderate–high).

**Translations.** 28:17 οἱ δὲ ἐδίστασαν: NASB95 "but some were doubtful"; ESV "but some doubted" (overview English table) `[T]`. 28:20 πάσας τὰς ἡμέρας: both "always" — the fourth "all" is lost in both versions `[T]`. **Pulpit divergence:** none between them; but neither version lets the congregation hear the fourfold πᾶς. Say it.

**Pitfall.** The overview's Trap 6 — "Go!" as the point. The only imperative is "make disciples", and it is framed by authority before it and presence after it. A second pitfall: omitting 28:17. The commission is given to worshippers some of whom doubted, which is the church as it is `[I]`. The corrective: preach authority, discipling, teaching to keep, and presence together, and include the doubters.

**Book-overview note:** confirms the inclusio, the mountain frame, Trap 6, the Dan 7:14 OG source of record, the διστάζω and προσέρχομαι chains. Extends with verified chains: βαπτίζω (3:6–16 ↔ 28:19 only), τηρέω (27:36, 54; 28:4 ↔ 28:20), συντέλεια τοῦ αἰῶνος (13:39–49; 24:3; 28:20), ὄνομα (1:21–25 ↔ 28:19).

## Cross-Passage Convergent Findings

These only become visible when the pericopes are read in sequence. **Every item here is synthetic.** Items marked *verified* have had every Greek reference checked by lemma or phrase against the SBLGNT index; for the Old Testament side, the check was against Swete, Rahlfs and the WLC. Items not marked verified are capped at moderate confidence. They are the first things a claim audit should test.

**1. The Sermon enacted in the passion — 5:21–48; 6:9–13 → 26:36–27:44.** *Verified; the design reading is moderate–high.*

- **The antitheses turned on Jesus.** ἔνοχος ("guilty", 5:21–22 → 26:66). ῥαπίζω ("to slap", 5:39 → 26:67). ὀμνύω ("to swear", 5:34 → 26:74). See Headline 1.
- **The Lord's Prayer prayed and mocked.** Several of its words return only in the passion:
  - γενηθήτω τὸ θέλημά σου ("your will be done", 6:10) is prayed at 26:42;
  - πειρασμός ("temptation", 6:13) is warned of at 26:41;
  - ῥύομαι ("deliver", 6:13) is mocked at 27:43, "let him deliver him now";
  - ἐν οὐρανῷ καὶ ἐπὶ γῆς ("in heaven and on earth", 6:10) returns, with the article, as the scope of authority at 28:18.
- **What the sequence shows.** The Sermon is not left behind when the teaching ends. Jesus keeps it under trial; every other party breaks it.

**2. The frame of the beginning — chapters 1–4 → 26–28.** *Verified.*

- **Six two-point chains.** ὄναρ ("dream", 1:20 … 27:19). Ναζωραῖος ("Nazarene", 2:23; 26:71). "King of the Jews" (2:2; 27:11–37). χαρὰ μεγάλη ("great joy", 2:10; 28:8). ἁγία πόλις ("holy city", 4:5; 27:53). The devil's "if" (4:3, 6; 27:40).
- **The mountain and the authority.** The mountain where the devil offered all the kingdoms (4:8–9) is answered by the mountain where all authority is given (28:16–18). ὄρος ὑψηλόν ("high mountain") occurs only at 4:8 and 17:1.
- **The presence.** The Immanuel inclusio is the book's frame: μεθ' ἡμῶν ("with us", 1:23) and μεθ' ὑμῶν ("with you", 28:20). It runs through 18:20 and 26:29, with μετ' ἐμοῦ ("with me") at 26:38, 40.
- **What the sequence shows.** Matthew builds his ending from the materials of his beginning. The confirmations and refinements of the overview's Christological Trajectory follow from this.

**3. The shepherd and the lost sheep — 2:6; 9:36; 10:6; 15:24; 18:12–14; 25:32; 26:31.** *Verified for the Greek; the Ezekiel link is moderate–high.*

- **The shepherd chain.** ποιμήν ("shepherd") occurs only at 9:36, 25:32 and 26:31. ποιμαίνω ("to shepherd") occurs at 2:6, from Mic 5:3 and 2 Sam 5:2.
- **Israel's sheep.** "Lost sheep of the house of Israel" occurs only at 10:6 and 15:24.
- **The straying one.** Swete's τὸ πλανώμενον ("the straying one", Ezek 34:4, 16) is Matthew's word at 18:12–13, against Luke's ἀπολωλός ("the lost one").
- **What the sequence shows.** The shepherd-king of 2:6 has compassion on a shepherdless crowd, sends to Israel's lost sheep, and seeks the straying one. He judges the nations as a shepherd separates sheep from goats, and is struck so that the sheep scatter. That is Ezekiel 34 and Zechariah 13 read together `[I]`, moderate–high.

**4. Discipleship called, undone and kept by others — 4:18–22 → 26:56–58 → 27:55–61 → 28:16–20.** *Verified.*

- **The call undone.** ἀφέντες ("leaving", 4:20, 22) returns at the arrest: ἀφέντες αὐτὸν πάντες ἔφυγον ("they all left Him and fled", 26:56).
- **Following.** ἀκολουθέω ("to follow") last describes a disciple at 26:58, where Peter follows "at a distance". It is next used of the women, at 27:55.
- **Serving.** διακονέω ("to serve") moves from the angels (4:11) to Peter's mother-in-law (8:15, "she served *him*", against Mark's "them"), to the Son of Man (20:28), the judgement (25:44) and the women (27:55).
- **Keeping watch.** γρηγορέω ("to keep watch", 24:42–25:13) fails at 26:38–41. τηρέω ("to keep watch over") is done by the guards (27:36, 54; 28:4) before it becomes the disciples' task with his words (28:20).
- **What the sequence shows.** The commission is given to men who left him, worshipping and some doubting (28:17). διστάζω ("to doubt") occurs only at 14:31 and 28:17.

**5. Blood, money and the people — 1:21; 23:35; 26:15, 28; 27:3–25; 28:12–15.** *Verified; see Headline 5.*

- **Matthew's own wording.** The sequence is built in his own words:
  - the forgiveness clause is moved from John's baptism to the cup;
  - ἀθῷος ("innocent") occurs only at 27:4 and 27:24;
  - σὺ ὄψῃ / ὑμεῖς ὄψεσθε ("see to that yourself / yourselves") passes the responsibility from hand to hand;
  - θόρυβος ("riot") occurs only at 26:5 and 27:24.
- **The silver.** The silver that bought the betrayal buys the silence about the resurrection.
- **What the sequence shows.** The book gives every party a share in handing over the innocent. It sets the cry of 27:25 inside the frame of 1:21 and 26:28. The overview's Trap 3 is answered from the text.

**6. The great reversals of judgement — 7:21–27 → 25:1–46.** *Verified.*

- **Wise and foolish.** φρόνιμος / μωρός ("wise" / "foolish") stand together only at 7:24–26 and 25:2–9.
- **"I do not know."** οὐκ οἶδα ("I do not know") is the bridegroom's verdict on the foolish (25:12). It is Peter's word at his denial (26:70, 72, 74). Compare οὐδέποτε ἔγνων ὑμᾶς ("I never knew you", 7:23).
- **Faithful slaves.** πιστός ("faithful") occurs only at 24:45 and 25:21–23.
- **The throne.** θρόνος δόξης ("throne of glory") occurs only at 19:28 and 25:31.
- **The evil eye.** ὀφθαλμὸς πονηρός ("evil eye") occurs only at 6:23 and 20:15.
- **What the sequence shows.** The Sermon's ending and the fifth discourse's ending are one judgement scene in two tellings. The passion then shows a disciple speaking the words of the condemned `[I]`, moderate.

**7. Matthew's Scriptures — the edition pattern.** *Verified against the printed editions only; see Headline 3.* Matthew stands with Rahlfs against Swete in five places, and often stands nearer the Hebrew than either. This is the most consequential method finding of the sweep, for two reasons. It bears on how every Greek-OT claim in the overview should be worded. And it means that the corpus's observation text for the Greek OT (Swete) is the wrong text to cite against Matthew without checking Rahlfs.

---

## Book-Overview Tensions

These are surfaced, not resolved. The overview is Draft v0.2. These are the points a Finalise pass should work through first.

**1. Add a fifth Swete–Rahlfs divergence (Isa 29:13 → 15:8), and state the pattern.** The overview's *How Matthew uses his Scriptures* names four divergences. Isa 29:13 is a fifth: Swete has ἐν τῷ στόματι αὐτοῦ ("with its mouth"), which Rahlfs and Matthew lack. **Recommend** that the overview state the pattern (Matthew with Rahlfs in all five), together with the caution about Rahlfs's eclectic text.

**2. 4:10 is not Matthew's own departure.** The overview calls 4:10 "one deliberate departure from every text". The same wording stands at Luke 4:8, so it belongs to the double tradition. The replacing verbs, προσκυνήσεις … λατρεύσῃς ("you shall worship … serve"), are the Decalogue's (Exod 20:5; Deut 5:9). **Recommend** rewording the row (unit 6).

**3. The Ps 78:2 row should read "independent of the LXX, nearer the Hebrew".** ἐρεύξομαι ("I will pour forth") is the Greek Psalter's own equivalent of נבע ("to pour forth") at Ps 19:3 (Swete 18:3). So the second line of 13:35 is a fresh rendering of the Hebrew, not "independent of both" (unit 26).

**4. 13:32 follows Theodotion.** The verbal match for the birds nesting in the branches is Theodotion Dan 4:18 (κατεσκήνουν … ἐν τοῖς κλάδοις, "nested … in the branches"), not the Old Greek (unit 26).

**5. The Zech 13:7 row at 26:31 needs qualifying.** The BHS apparatus cites Greek manuscripts reading πατάξω ("I will strike") and names Mt 26,31. So the first person is not purely "the Hebrew against both Greek texts". The singular is with the Hebrew (unit 50).

**6. The Isaiah 42 row (12:18–21) should say that εἰς νῖκος ("to victory", 12:20) matches neither Isaiah text.** Hab 1:4 (εἰς νῖκος, Swete) is a candidate source, *uncertain* (unit 23).

**7. The Tamar note in the Echo Table overstates the text.** Gen 38 gives no ethnicity for Tamar (WLC and Swete). Her being Canaanite is an inference from the setting and from later tradition `[S]`, and the row should say so (unit 1).

**9. Echo Table: verified chains to add.** These are grouped by where they land. All are SBLGNT, verified by lemma or phrase, and none is yet in the overview's table.

- **The infancy and the passion:** ὄναρ (1:20 → 27:19); Ναζωραῖος (2:23 ↔ 26:71); ἡγούμενος (2:6) with 2 Sam 5:2 / 1 Chr 11:2; ἀργύριον (26:15 → 28:12–15).
- **The Sermon and the passion:** ἔνοχος (5:21–22 ↔ 26:66); ὀμνύω (5:34 ↔ 26:74); ἀρνέομαι (10:33 ↔ 26:70–72); ψευδομαρτυρία (15:19 ↔ 26:59); ῥύομαι (6:13 → 27:43); ἐν οὐρανῷ καὶ ἐπὶ γῆς (6:10 → 28:18).
- **Within the teaching:** καταλύω (5:17 → 26:61; 27:40); 5:18 ↔ 24:34–35; ὀφθαλμὸς πονηρός (6:23 ↔ 20:15); λίθος + ἄρτος (4:3 → 7:9); 3:10 = 7:19; ἐπὶ τῇ διδαχῇ αὐτοῦ (7:28 → 22:33); φορτίον (11:30 ↔ 23:4); πραΰς (5:5; 11:29; 21:5); νήπιος (11:25 ↔ 21:16); ἀποκαλύπτω (10:26 → 11:25, 27 → 16:17); ἐλεάω (5:7 → 18:33); λύω (16:19 ↔ 18:18); τέλειος (5:48 ↔ 19:21); μεταμέλομαι (21:29, 32 ↔ 27:3).
- **The disciples:** ἀφέντες (4:20–22 ↔ 26:56); διακονέω (8:15 → 27:55); γρηγορέω (24:42 → 26:41); οὐκ οἶδα (25:12 ↔ 26:70–74); ἑταῖρος ("friend", 20:13; 22:12; 26:50); ῥαββί (only Judas addresses Jesus so, 26:25, 49).
- **The mission and the end:** "lost sheep" (10:6 ↔ 15:24); ποιμήν (9:36 → 25:32 → 26:31); πάντα τὰ ἔθνη (24:9, 14; 25:32; 28:19); βαπτίζω (chapter 3 ↔ 28:19 only); τηρέω (27:36, 54; 28:4 ↔ 28:20); συντέλεια τοῦ αἰῶνος (13:39–49; 24:3; 28:20); ἐπισυνάγω (23:37 → 24:31); ἀπ' ἄρτι (23:39; 26:29, 64).
- **The passion:** ἐκχυννόμενον (23:35 → 26:28); θόρυβος (26:5 ↔ 27:24); λῃστής (21:13 → 27:44); right and left (20:21, 23 → 27:38); κάλαμος (12:20 → 27:29–48); κεφαλή (8:20 → 27:29–39); ξένος (25:35–44 ↔ 27:7); εὐχαριστέω (15:36 ↔ 26:27); θέλημα / γενηθήτω (6:10 → 26:42).
- **The resurrection:** διαφημίζω (9:31 ↔ 28:15); ἀστραπή (24:27 ↔ 28:3); ἠγέρθη ἀπὸ τῶν νεκρῶν (14:2; 27:64; 28:7); χαρά (2:10 → 28:8); πιστός (24:45 ↔ 25:21–23); κράσπεδον (9:20; 14:36; 23:5).

**10. Structural refinements.**

- **The two hinges have a third member.** ἀπὸ τότε ("from then on") marks the preaching (4:17), the passion (16:21) and the betrayer's beginning (26:16). The overview treats 26:16 as an addendum; the sweep would make it a structural third, *moderate* (unit 49).
- **Herod.** The overview pairs Herod with Pilate, but Ἡρῴδης ("Herod") occurs only in chapters 2 and 14. Add the pattern ἀκούσας δέ … ἀνεχώρησεν ("having heard … he withdrew", 2:22; 4:12; 14:13) (unit 28).
- **The parable links.** The verbatim links 21:34 = 22:3 and 21:36 = 22:4 bind the three parables of judgement (unit 42).
- **All the nations.** The overview's note that "all the nations" is kept for the last line needs qualifying. The idea is present at 24:9, 14 and 25:32 (unit 41).

**11. The English tables.**

- **9:13; 12:7.** The NASB95's "compassion" breaks the ἔλεος ("mercy") chain and collides with σπλαγχνίζομαι ("felt compassion", 9:36).
- **9:22.** Both versions turn σῴζω ("to save") into "made well".
- **18:6–9.** The ESV's "sin" and "temptations" hide σκανδαλίζω ("to cause to stumble").
- **26:64.** NASB95 "hereafter" against ESV "from now on".
- **26:66.** Both versions lose the link to "guilty" at 5:22.
- **28:20.** Both versions lose the fourth "all".

**12. A textual note to add.**

- **17:20.** ὀλιγοπιστία ("little faith") is the NA28 reading; the Majority text has ἀπιστία ("unbelief"). The "little faith" thread should say so (unit 35).
- **19:9.** The SBLGNT and NA28 differ on the second clause (unit 38).

## Preaching Pitfalls (book level)

The overview's seven traps were confirmed from the text at every place they bite. Three book-level pitfalls emerged from reading the units in sequence.

**1. Preaching the passion without the Sermon.** The passion narratives are often preached as atonement theology alone. Matthew tells them in the Sermon's vocabulary: the oath, the slap, the false witness, the prayer, the will. *Corrective:* in a series, let 26–27 recall 5–7 explicitly. The one who taught the Sermon is the one who keeps it.

**2. Preaching the disciples as the church's heroes, or its foils.** The Eleven leave him (26:56). Then they are sent, worshipping and doubting (28:17). Women follow and serve at the cross (27:55). Gentiles confess him (27:54). *Corrective:* preach the commission as given to the restored, not the reliable.

**3. Preaching the formula citations with the English Old Testament only.** Matthew's wording often stands with the Hebrew, or with one Greek edition against another (Headline 3). An English OT reference can make Matthew look careless or creative where he is following a text. *Corrective:* when a formula citation is preached, say which text Matthew's wording follows, without claiming more than the printed editions show.

---

## Open Questions / Uncertainties

1. **27:9–10, "Jeremiah".** The crux stands; see the overview's checking list. The sweep adds nothing.
2. **The Swete–Rahlfs pattern.** Is Matthew's agreement with Rahlfs a fact about Matthew's Greek Bible, or about Rahlfs's choice of manuscripts? The question needs the Göttingen apparatus, which the folder does not hold. Until then, the finding is about the printed editions.
3. **27:52–53, the raised saints.** The sweep reads their function in context. It does not settle whether the scene is narrated as event, as apocalyptic sign, or both `[S]`.
4. **16:18–19.** The sweep confirms the Isa 22:22 link from the Hebrew. It does not settle the Petrine-office question (Trap 5).
5. **25:31–46, "the least of these my brothers".** The sweep adds the ξένος ("stranger") chain to 27:7. It does not settle the identity question (Trap 7).
6. **Ezek 34 and Zech 13.** Is the shepherd sequence (Convergent 3) a design, or a set of separate inherited links? This is for an audit.
7. **The ⭐ units among the briefer ones** (22, 23, 29, 37, 40, 46, 48, 51, 53–56) need solo digs before the pulpit more than the others do.

---

## Checking Stage — the July 2026 Sweep

The July sweep (`dig-deeper-matthew-whole-book.md`) was opened only after all 56 units and the sections above were complete.

**Where it agrees.** Its headline findings are all confirmed from the text:

- the Immanuel inclusio;
- fulfilment as the authorial spine;
- disciple-making as the book's arc;
- the gradual unveiling from Son of David to universal Lord;
- the Servant as the key to suffering and mission.

**Where this sweep goes further.** The July sweep worked the sixteen tools at book level and did not work pericope by pericope. It therefore has none of the following:

- the verified two-point chains;
- the Swete–Rahlfs pattern;
- the nearer-the-Hebrew departures;
- the passion's enactment of the Sermon.

**Where they pull differently.**

- **The source of tensions.** The July sweep's tensions are with a commentary introduction (Cooper). Those were not retested here, and remain open for the passages they concern.
- **The fulfilment spine.** The July sweep's Headline 2 speaks of "the ~10–12 formula quotations". The overview and this sweep count ten in the narrator's voice. Jesus' own ἀναπληροῦται ("is fulfilled", 13:14) stands outside them.

**Conclusion.** Nothing in the July sweep contradicts a finding here. The July sweep can be retired as a working document. It remains in the folder as a record.

---

## Text-First Declaration

**Mode:** Multi-Passage Sweep. Fifty-six pericopes, following the overview's 56 preaching units exactly.

**Primary texts actually opened:**

- **SBLGNT** `greek-nt-sblgnt/01-Matt.txt`. Every unit was read in Greek before any English was consulted.
- **The MorphGNT lemma index** `_index/01-Matt.tsv`. It was used for every count and chain.
- **The SBLGNT index files for Mark and Luke.** Used for the Synoptic checks, and for New Testament-wide uniqueness claims (for example μαθητεύω, "to make a disciple").
- **NA28** `01-Matthew-NA28.txt` and `01-Matthew-NA28-apparatus.txt`. Opened at the brackets and apparatus entries cited in the units, including 1:7–8, 10; 16:2b–3; 17:20; 19:9; 21:44; 27:16–17; 27:49. The parser was extended during the run to recover 12:32 and 15:8–9, and now yields 1,068 verses.
- **Swete** (including Theodotion Daniel and Susanna), **Rahlfs–Hanhart text exports**, and the **WLC** with `tools/find.py`. Opened at every direct quotation and at each allusion candidate named above.
- **The BHS text and apparatus exports.** Opened at Zech 13:7, among other places.
- **The NASB95 and ESV exports.** Every English quotation was copied from them.

**Editions named:**

- Every Greek NT count is stated against the **SBLGNT**.
- Every Greek OT wording is stated against **Swete**, with **Rahlfs** where they differ.
- Every Hebrew form is stated against the **WLC**.
- **There is no LXX apparatus in the folder.** No finding rests on an LXX variant.

**Automated check of counts.** After the units were written, a script extracted every claim of the form "X occurs (only) at …" and re-checked it against the SBLGNT index, by lemma or by phrase.

| Result | Claims |
|---|---|
| Passed | 136 |
| Not machine-checkable (multi-word or paraphrased claims) | 141 |
| Flagged | 25 |

Of the 25 flagged, 23 were parsing artefacts. **Two were real errors, and both are corrected in the text:**

- **Unit 8.** παρακαλέω ("to comfort") "only at 2:18 and 5:4" is true of the passive only. The active stands in 9 verses.
- **Unit 11.** ἐν οὐρανῷ καὶ ἐπὶ γῆς ("in heaven and on earth") stands only at 6:10. The form with the article (ἐπὶ τῆς γῆς) stands at 28:18.

Several other counts were corrected by hand during the run: units 15, 16, 22, 24, 32, 39, 48, 51 and 55.

**Secondary sources present in context:**

- Overview v0.2 and its claim audit, both from this project and by the same model (see the independence caveat in *How to Read*). `[S: audit]` marks every claim taken from the audit without re-derivation.
- The Mark sweep and the Mark claim audit, used as the format model and for Amos 8:9 / Exod 10:22 at 27:45.

Three passages in units 52, 53 and 56 first tagged as audited were corrected before delivery: Mic 5:1 (27:30), Deut 27:25 / 2 Sam 3:28 / Sus Th 46 (27:24–25) and Hag 1:13 / Gen 28:15 (28:20) are audit candidates still in the queue, not audited claims.

No commentary or monograph was opened. Every `[S]` item is from general knowledge of the literature, and none is load-bearing.

**Warrant tags in this report:** `[T]` 2694 · `[I]` 512 · `[S]` 81 · `[S: audit]` 64. Composite tags such as `[T]`/`[I]` are counted under each.

**Tools worked before secondary sources were consulted:** confirmed for every pericope. The overview's specific claims were checked only after each unit's Greek work.

**Passage text:** verified. Every Greek quotation is copied from the corpus files, not reconstructed. SBLGNT sigla are removed from the quotations.
